| T2.1 | Host-speed probe (single-thread loop + null-kernel burst), adapting `scripts/host_cpu_probe.py` | Code | | Runs on CPU; GPU path dry-run tested | Done (25 Oct 2026): `host_speed.py` in `attnbench/components/`, run by `host_cpu_probe.py speed`. A fixed pure-Python loop, then bursts of the null kernel `torch.cuda._sleep(0)` timed launch by launch, with the device synchronised outside the timer before each burst; CPU clock and steal over the probe; one provenance-stamped row per tag, and a tag is never reused. Runs on CPU; the GPU path is dry-run tested with fakes. The session and transfer bounds are set at lock (T3.2, T4.1) |
| T2.2 | Null-kernel host-component profiler (adapting TaxBreak's protocol to the whole operation sequence); also profiles the reference mask builder on the target host and banks the profile, under both instruments, replacing the unbanked figures in Sections 1 and 2 (Appendix A, 19 and 27 Oct 2026) | Code | T2.3 | Unit-tested; L4 smoke run | Done (9 Nov 2026), on this evidence (adviser's ruling 4 of the eleventh 9 Nov 2026 paste, `docs/prereqs_20261109.md`): `host_components` passed on the L4 in T3.2a to T3.2d (T3.1's step 5; T3.2d 79 min on identity `3c44588`; ledger rows of 7 to 9 Nov 2026), the L4 run its acceptance names. Before that: Partly built (27 Oct 2026): `host_profile.py` and `builder_profile.py` in `attnbench/components/`, the second run by `profile_reference_builder.py` in `scripts/`. **The null pass** runs the arm's prefill from the tape (T2.4, so it waits for nothing and takes the recorded path) with a dispatch mode that replaces each aten call that launches device work by one null kernel (`torch.cuda._sleep(0)`, T2.1's) and an output allocated at the shape the call would give, and replaces the block-sparse entry point the same way. Per launching call it times the gap (the arm's Python and the dispatcher since the previous call) and the launch; calls that launch nothing run for real; host-compute regions are timed directly and taken out of the gap, so they are charged once. Costs go to recorded positions by runs of one aten operator in one layer: the first operation of a call carries gap + launch and each further one a median null launch, and a run that is not a multiple of the launching calls, an entry that was not nulled, a call out of order, a surplus call, a marker count that differs and a CUDA-graph launch are all refused (`ProfileMismatch`), so no cost goes to a different sequence. The profile is the per-position median over repetitions with every repetition kept (for T2.19's bootstrap), and `host_costs` is `to_operations`' `host` argument. Tested on CPU with a scripted clock, hand-computed, with a mutant for each guard. **The reference builder** is profiled on the host it runs on, by two instruments banked side by side, because they disagree: `cProfile` does not count a tensor `__setitem__` or `__getitem__` (a type slot) as a call, so the builder's ~100,000 aten calls per build at 32768 land in its own time, while `torch.profiler` sees them as operators. A development run on a Mac (not the target host, not banked) gave `cProfile` 94.2% at 32768 (0.374 s of 0.397 s per 10 calls, the plan's figure to the digit), `torch.profiler` 0.53 and a lower bound of 0.28 (one minus its operator time over the unprofiled wall time); a loop of tensor slot writes reads 1.00 and 0.51. So the plan's "about 94% is Python overhead" holds under `cProfile` and does not under `torch.profiler`, and the 19 Oct 2026 line does not say which instrument it was. Decided by the researcher (Appendix A, 27 Oct 2026): Sections 1 and 2 state the share per instrument, `cProfile` and `torch.profiler`, and the builder is profiled at density 0.5, the script's default. Not done: the L4 smoke run, and the arm runners that supply `run_prefill`, the tape and the recording (T2.27 step 1); a Triton entry point is not nulled yet (XAttention on the A100 and H100), and a compiled arm's graph launch is refused (T2.21). Known limits of the instrument, to be checked on the L4: the tape's `TorchFunctionMode` adds a little to a gap that a real run does not have, and the replaced call skips the kernel's C++ front end, so a gap can be a little long and a launch a little short; when the calls of one run launch different numbers of kernels the cost is spread evenly inside the run. Shakedown 2's `t22` (28 Oct 2026, built, not run) takes the null-kernel profile of mean-pool's prefill from its tape on the L4 host, profiles the reference builder under both instruments there, and reads the 32768 row against the 94%. **Bare-timer prices** (researcher's ruling, 1 Nov 2026, after Shakedown 5; built 1 Nov 2026, `bare_timer.py` in `attnbench/components/`): the prediction's host costs are each call's bare time, for leaves and composites alike, re-issued alone at its prefill inputs at least 1000 times after a synchronise outside the timer, the median less the timer's own overhead; a composite whose every position a child launched is priced by its children (the chain), checked against its own bare time in aggregate (T4.1 row 32), and a cell that fails takes each composite's own time, labelled, and is not refused. The null pass stays as the instrumented comparison and the instrumented/bare ratio is recorded per call, per operator and per cell. Shakedown 6's `pricing_zoo` (t228) runs it on every arm, the oracle included. Shakedown 6 (1 Nov 2026): the oracle aligned and passed the bare chain gate at 5.5% with the entry fix, instrumented/bare 11.5 (dense at 8192: 5.5). XAttention's null pass was refused at a `where` with a Python number. Built after it, on the researcher's rulings of 1 Nov 2026: once-only host pricing (`align_calls`), tested on Shakedown 6's sequence with a conservation test; the per-layer residual added back to the bare prices (`layer_residual.py`, T4.1 item 34); and the null pass sped up (the residual measured with the device idle since 8 Nov 2026, Appendix A). The isolated trace is read by bisection (the oracle at 8192 took 4 s, down from over 27 minutes), each nulled call's output shape is cached by signature, and an operator no chain names returns at once, so a pass takes about half its former time. Step 5 records each phase's seconds per cell. |

| T2.6 | Launch-queue depth probe | Code | | Q measured on the L4 | Done (9 Nov 2026), on this evidence (adviser's ruling 4 of the eleventh 9 Nov 2026 paste, `docs/prereqs_20261109.md`): Q measured on the L4: 1022 in all five warm settings in Shakedown 2 (ledger row of 31 Oct 2026), the warm probe the method of record (Appendix A, 31 Oct 2026). Before that: Partly built (25 Oct 2026): `launch_queue.py` in `attnbench/components/`, run by `scripts/measure_launch_queue.py`. A long spin kernel, then short kernels launched behind it and timed on the host; the first launch that waits for the spin marks a full queue, so Q is the number of operations in flight, the spin included, which is the Q of the recursion's D_(i-Q). Each trial that never fills the queue gives only a lower bound, and `queue_depth` refuses trials that disagree. Tested on CPU against a simulated queue that the recursion's queue term reproduces; Q is measured on the L4 (T2.27). Two kernels are probed, the null kernel and a one-element add, because a launch's queue footprint can depend on its arguments; the model's Q is the null kernel's (Appendix A, 25 Oct 2026). Shakedown 1 (27 Oct 2026) gave the null kernel 1022 in all five trials and the add `1,1022,1022,1022,1022`, so no agreed Q and a lower bound of 1: the first trial's first launch waited, which looks like a one-off kernel load. The warm probe (one completed launch of each kind before its blocker) is tested on a simulated queue whose first launch loads. Shakedown 2 (31 Oct 2026) gave the add `1,1022,1022,1022,1022` cold again and 1022 warm in all five warm settings, equal to the null kernel. The warm probe is the method of record and cold is a diagnostic (`--cold`) (Appendix A, 31 Oct 2026) |

Only the separate nsys-traced pass of the L4 shakedown may claim no page faults, and only for the arms it traced, and only when `scripts/um_fault_control.py`, run under the same nsys options in that session, recorded faults. | T2.12 | Precomputed-mask ablation arm | Code | | Passes selector gate | Done (9 Nov 2026), on this evidence (adviser's ruling 4 of the eleventh 9 Nov 2026 paste, `docs/prereqs_20261109.md`): Shakedown 2's `t212` (the real kernel, logits and executed density against the source arm) passed on the L4 in Shakedowns 4 and 5 (ledger rows of 1 Nov 2026, `a0b04da` and `b13ef8b`); the selector gate passes (below). It computes no score, builds no mask and reads nothing from the host. Batch 1 only (the kernel call's limit, as XAttention's), so the batch-4 cells have no precomputed-mask arm (Appendix A, 27 Oct 2026). In the ground-truth harness it is a new arm kind that is labelled an ablation, is refused any scoring call or mask build in any phase, and is refused a prompt other than its own. Tested: the kernel receives the recorded masks unchanged and without a per-forward copy; a replay reproduces its source arm's output; the stored per-head masks equal the head-uniform mask bitwise when heads share scores (the selector gate, Section 5.5); a set cannot be replayed on another prompt. Every check has been watched to fail under a mutation (27 Oct 2026). Still to do: the same on a GPU with the real kernel (L4 dry run; Shakedown 2's `t212` is built, 28 Oct 2026, and not run: it records mean-pool's and XAttention's masks, replays them through the real kernel, compares logits and executed density, and times the source and precomputed arms together), where the replay is expected to match its source arm bitwise for XAttention; it is not yet known to for mean-pool, whose banked path leaves the kernel's `deterministic` flag at its default. The arm is not registered with the backend registry, since it is bound to one prompt's masks |
| T2.13 | Untraced ground-truth harness: arms interleaved, except compiled variants, which run one per process (Appendix A, 27 Oct 2026); dense control, traced-vs-untraced overhead, median, p90 and p99, GPU clock and throttle sampling; records the timestamp of the first timed block, which the late-prediction check reads (Appendix A, 25 Oct 2026). Every deployable arm's timed region includes its estimator, mask build, copies and synchronisations; the oracle arm may use cached scores and is labelled a non-deployable reference (scoring cost outside the timed path) (Appendix A, 26 Oct 2026; label corrected 27 Oct 2026) (adapt `run_vectorised_endtoend.py` / `phase_timing.py`, both of which compute scores before the clock starts) | Code | | L4 dry run; an acceptance test fails if a deployable arm's scores are computed outside the timed block | Done (9 Nov 2026), on this evidence (adviser's ruling 4 of the eleventh 9 Nov 2026 paste, `docs/prereqs_20261109.md`): the eager ground truth ran on the L4 in the exploratory session and T3.2a to T3.2d, every group complete in T3.2d (step 9; ledger rows of 7 to 9 Nov 2026); the compiled groups are not in P2 unless resolved by 14 Nov 2026 (adviser's ruling 2 of the tenth 9 Nov 2026 paste). Before that: Partly built (27 Oct 2026; follow-ups 28 Oct 2026); it has run only against fakes on a CPU (its dry-run step also runs on a CPU, on the dense arm with a scripted reader): `attnbench/ground_truth.py` and `attnbench/clock_sampling.py`. Arms are timed interleaved, in an order that rotates each round, with the device synchronised on both sides of each timed block; the median, p90 and p99 and the start of the first ground-truth block are recorded (a rerun never moves it; tested against `load_committed`'s late-prediction refusal, which refuses a prediction synced at or after the stamp). Under the strict rule (Appendix A, 27 Oct 2026) the stamp is the start of the first clocked block of any kind, the dense control's clocked warm-up included, so a prediction synced during that warm-up is late; built 28 Oct 2026, and the result key is now `first_clocked_block_started_at` (`check_control` of T2.16 reads the same stamp). A probe logs every scoring call and mask build; a deployable arm is refused if any ran before the clock started or if a timed block computed no scores, and the oracle's rows carry the label. Exactly one dense control is required. Its warm-up time is the median of its clocked warm-up forwards after the cold first one, and a round whose dense time differs from that by more than the resolution floor, in either direction, is flagged. GPU clocks and throttle reasons are read around every timed block, outside the clock, from NVML's cumulative power and thermal violation counters, because an instantaneous read after the closing synchronise sees only an idle GPU; a reader without those counters needs an in-block sampling thread and is refused without one. Which reasons flag a block is set per session and has no default in the code; the researcher's set (Appendix A, 27 Oct 2026) is hardware slowdown, the two thermal slowdowns and the hardware power brake, and the software power cap, normal on an L4 under load, is recorded and never flags. A flagged round is excluded from every statistic and rerun within a stated budget, and a result with too few clean rounds says `complete: false`. `time_with_profiler_overhead` measures the untraced ground truth first and the traced run second, in that order, and reports the per-arm overhead against the resolution floor. An arm names its compile setting, the harness checks it against the backend, and a second compiled setting in one process is refused. Every check has been watched to fail under a mutation (27 Oct 2026). The session's detector is a separate sampler process reading the throttle-reason bits at about 10 Hz (`ProcessSampler`, built 28 Oct 2026, tested on a scripted card), because hardware slowdown has no counter and the power counter holds the software cap and the hardware brake together. The rule is on the bits alone: `RESEARCHER_FLAG_BITS` (hardware slowdown, both thermal slowdowns, the hardware power brake) flags and the software power cap is recorded and never flags. Each block is judged on the samples inside it and the one either side, so a block shorter than the interval is still bracketed; a dead, stalled or unbuildable sampler stops the session. The in-process sampler's counters flag only if every bit a counter integrates is in the set, which closes the open point about the power counter. The session's controls refuse a counters-only sampler and any flag set missing one of the four bits above (31 Oct 2026). Still owed: running it. Shakedown 2 (`scripts/t228_steps.py`, launch card `docs/t228_launch_card.md`) has the steps, built 28 Oct 2026 and not run: `nvml` (the real NVML reader and the sampler process against a card) and `t213` (the harness on the shakedown arms, the stamp, the sampler's own cost on a host-bound arm, the traced overhead, `check_control`). The resolution floor is the researcher's at lock, and the dry run uses a provisional one. On P2 and P4 (9 Nov 2026) the session runs this harness through T3.1's ground-truth step, on the pairing's config or on one block's grid (`t61_session.real_harness`), tested on CPU only |

The queue sensitivity re-runs a committed file's own components at each multiple of Q and with no queue term, and names the depths at which the sparse-versus-dense decision would flip. | T2.16 | Dense-baseline selection (K2): candidate kernels, correctness and fault check, warm-up timing, recorded choice; restate banked A100 figures from measurements | Code | | Tested on CPU fakes; L4 dry run | Done (9 Nov 2026), on this evidence (adviser's ruling 4 of the eleventh 9 Nov 2026 paste, `docs/prereqs_20261109.md`): `dense_select` passed on the L4 in T3.2a to T3.2d (step 3; ledger rows of 7 to 9 Nov 2026); the FA3 backend below is T6.4's prerequisite, not P2's. Before that: Partly built (27 Oct 2026): `attnbench/dense_selection.py`, tested on CPU fakes with the real correctness gate. One call per cell (card, context length, batch): each planned candidate (SDPA flash, cuDNN, memory-efficient, FA2, and FA3 on the H100) is declined where its own capability says it faults the device, and is then never launched; checked with `gates.check_for_family`, which uses the float64 reference where it fits and agreement among the other candidates where it does not, and the check kind is named per candidate; and timed in interleaved warm-up rounds. The choice is the lowest median among candidates that were correct and ran every round, with every candidate within the resolution floor of it listed. A planned candidate that was not supplied makes the record `complete: false`, and `check_control` refuses it. The record is written once with a sha256 sidecar, and loading recomputes every median from its samples and the choice from the medians. `check_control` requires the dense control to be the recorded choice, the record complete, and the record made before the first timed block. `check_control` takes the prediction key (or its K2 cell) and refuses a choice recorded for another card, context length or batch (Appendix A, 31 Oct 2026). `measured_restatement` states a speedup only from one session in which the dense arm is the chosen kernel; `arithmetic_restatement` reproduces the banked A100 caveat's 1.072x, 1.180x and 1.260x at 16384 from the parquets and labels them arithmetic. | T2.22 | Dispatcher and its comparators (kernel-speedup rule, always dense, best-kernel sparse arm, nearest pairing); regret and decision errors, with and without the Holm-corrected admissible set; margin-against-speedup frontier; choice written into the hashed prediction file | Code | T2.8, T2.11, T2.19 | Tests pass on synthetic grids | Done (27 Oct 2026): `dispatcher.py` in `attnbench/predict/`, tested on a hand-worked grid, with a mutant for each rule and guard. The dispatcher takes the admissible configuration with the shortest predicted time and dense unless it is faster by more than the resolution floor (Section 5.9; exactly the floor is dense). The comparators are the kernel-speedup rule, always dense, one fixed best-kernel configuration and the nearest pairing's best measured choice. Scoring is on the measured grid at 16384 and 32768: regret (mean and worst), decision errors by the `scoring.py` outcomes with cells inside the floor not interpreted, and time gained against always-dense; `dense_best_everywhere` marks the case where always-dense has zero regret. Every admissible set is evaluated by label, so the uncorrected and the Holm-corrected sets sit side by side, and dropping an arm from a set removes it from the choices and from the reference (the per-arm gate rule, Section 5.1). `margin_frontier` sweeps the accuracy margin (admissible iff the lower bound is above minus the margin, the rule `paired_exact_ni` applies, checked against it) and reports the speedup each margin buys. The choices of the dispatcher and of each comparator are written into the hashed prediction file with their inputs, and the analysis recomputes every one from the file's own predictions and refuses a file whose choices do not follow (K9). Accepted by the researcher (Appendix A, 27 Oct 2026): ties among equal predicted times, or equal kernel speedups, go to the smaller (arm, density); "always the sparse arm with the best kernel speedup" is read as one fixed configuration, the one with the best geometric-mean kernel speedup over the points where it is admissible, left unscored where a point lacks it. Proposed, awaiting the researcher: the dispatcher decides per model, context length and batch; an arm on two code paths at one point is refused unless the deployed code path is named. Owed from elsewhere: the Holm-corrected set, which the admissibility analysis produces once its family is declared at lock (T4.1), and the real kernel times, which are device components from the T2.4 replays |

It reads a committed file alone: an arm is committed at several densities, each with the device costs T2.4 replayed from its own recorded masks, so each position's host, link, device and synchronisation cost is interpolated linearly in density between the two nearest committed densities (extrapolated from the nearest two outside the range, and flagged), and the recursion and every other predictor that reads only the components are re-run at the committed Q. The learned predictor and the nearest-pairing baseline cannot be recomputed from the file and are listed, as is a family with one density, with different operation sequences, with a repeated density, a density outside (0, 1], an interpolated component below zero, and an arm with no measured difference; nothing is assumed zero. `nonlinearity` reports how far the middle densities sit from the chord, since linear is an assumption. `decision_flips` names the offsets at which the predicted sign of sparse minus dense changes (a tie is dense). Proposed, awaiting the researcher: the difference is an absolute change in mask density by arm or by arm and length, supplied by the mask gate (T2.23, not built), and the abscissa is the key's density unless the measured mask density of each cell is given. **T2.27 checklist: the L4 shakedown session.** One session on the L4 in `asia-northeast1-a`. Each step is recorded as passed, or as failed with its fix, and every row is banked with `git_dirty = False`. Each task's own "Done when" still decides that task.

1. T2.2: smoke run of the null-kernel host-component profiler; profile the reference mask builder on the L4's host and bank the profile.
2. T2.3: record traces of two code paths, with the GPU stream IDs used, for the sequence checker's tests.
3. T2.4: replay each arm's kernel sequence as a CUDA graph with recorded masks, and run the replay-versus-trace check end to end. This is a dry run; the check of record is P1's, in T3.1.
4. T2.5: the link microbenchmarks, every field in one provenance-stamped row.
5. T2.6: measure the launch-queue depth Q.
6. T2.13: dry run of the untraced ground-truth harness.
7. T2.16: dry run of the dense-baseline selection.
8. T2.17: the asserts-removed XAttention arm's per-session checks, and its bitwise mask and output gate against the shipped arm.
9. T2.21: dry run of the compiled variants.
10. T2.7: check whether nsys is on the image (`check_trace.py nsys-available`). If it is not, install it in this session and record that here. Then, in a separate traced pass that is never a timing run, run `um_fault_control.py` and the arms under the same nsys options, and run `check_trace.py nsys-faults` on each export. "No page faults" holds only for the arms that pass traced, and only if the control faulted.

| T3.1 | P1 session: components, committed prediction, ground truth; GCP, launch by 9 Dec 2026 | Code prepares; R runs | Every Phase 2 task Built or Done, except T2.14's AWS half, T2.22 and T2.23 to T2.26; T2.27 Done | Rows banked | Done (9 Nov 2026), on this evidence (adviser's ruling 4 of the eleventh 9 Nov 2026 paste, `docs/prereqs_20261109.md`): P1's rows are banked from T3.2a to T3.2d (`results/t32a_p1_20261107T1640Z/` to `results/t32d_p1_20261109T0726Z/`; ledger rows of 7 to 9 Nov 2026), T3.2d's steps 1 to 12 passing on identity `3c44588`; the "not run on a GPU" below predates them. Before that: Built (31 Oct 2026), not run on a GPU: the session runner `scripts/t31_session.py`, its steps `scripts/t31_steps.py`, the config `configs/t31/p1_session.json` and the launch card `docs/t31_launch_card.md` (cap ₹760: a 7 h halt at ₹108.64/h). It runs the twelve steps in order (the twelfth, Gate A's delay injection, since 8 Nov 2026, Appendix A), stops at the first failure, logs each step and resumes from a named step. All twelve are wired: `probe` checks T2.18's extension against P1's own full recording at every grid length and keeps the full-length structure where they differ; `host_components` records each cell (arm, density, length, batch) and takes its T2.2 null-kernel profile; `device_components` replays each recording as a CUDA graph (live masks checked against the bank), prices substituted positions from the original operators, and joins the host, device and link costs into the recursion's operations, with work outside the decoder layers given a step of its own; `ground_truth` runs each group in its own process, the K2 choice as the dense control (checked with `check_control`), the group's arms at every density, compiled under the group's one setting. The undecided values come from `docs/lock_value_table.md` only, marked "working, pre-lock", and are refused for any pairing but P1. The floors and the rerun budget stay null as inputs: `dense_select` derives a session floor from a dense pre-pass at the provisional quantile, saving the series for the lock, and nothing is rerun. The XAttention arms run at batch 1 only and their cell density is the mask density they produce. `hold` waits for the sync time plus `PLANNED_SYNC_WAIT_S` (90 s); `verify` runs `load_committed` against the store with an empty `banked_at`. Tested on a CPU dry run and with the GPU pieces faked at their module boundaries. Second session 2 Nov 2026 (₹22, zone c after a zone-a stockout): step 1 stopped the session because memcheck's positive control saw no error, the read landing in a neighbouring allocation; memcheck now pads every allocation. Every other part of the contract passed, and the padding changed nothing bitwise on the synthetic masks and on all 28 layers of the real `qa_1` prompt, so the `qa_1` re-check is dropped (Appendix A, 2 Nov 2026). Third session 2 Nov 2026 (₹270): steps 1 to 4 passed, the kernel contract clean on the L4; step 5 was killed by the out-of-memory killer at 31.6 GB in its 32nd cell (`oracle_d0.5_L32768`) after 31 of 36 cells. Step 5 now runs each cell in its own process under an RSS ceiling and keeps the isolated replay as a compact event stream (Appendix A, 2 Nov 2026); the next session starts from step 1 and reruns all of step 5. Next (7 Nov 2026, researcher's ruling 4 of 6 Nov): an exploratory session, `docs/t31_exploratory_card.md` (live from step 1, fixes resumed on the instance, labelled "T3.1 exploratory, code identity may change", cap ₹500, `oracle_d0.5_L32768` not measured), then T3.2a and T3.2b as the data of record. ## 9. Known risks

- **Small panel.** Seven pairings; claims are limited to the measured host platforms and GPUs.
- **Limits stated in advance.** Batch 1 and 4 only, with no extrapolation to larger batches. One CUDA stream. G3 is a controlled study scoped to the tested models, tasks and block sizes, not a general law. The chunked-prefill extension is not representative of state-of-the-art chunked prefill.
- **Structural probe may miss length-dependent branches.** Checked by agreement between its two short contexts; a disagreement means the arm's sequence is taken as "different" for H0 and H2.
- **Separability may fail** (Gate D).
- **The whole-process `-O` control is not run** (Appendix A, 12 Oct 2026), so no claim is made about the size of `-O`'s effect on the rest of the process. The isolation of the asserts-removed arm rests on what the instrument does, verified in every session, not on an estimate of what it leaves behind: the hook compiles exactly one package with asserts stripped and logs every module it compiled; the bytecode check confirms that only assert sequences and `__debug__` blocks disappeared from that package; the hook's log matches the `xattn` package and `sys.flags.optimize` is 0, so every other module keeps its asserts; and the default arm is checked to still carry XAttention's asserts.
- **One code-path mismatch with three faces.** XAttention's thresholds were calibrated on the A100's Triton path (T1.1 amendment A4) and are applied on the L4's PyTorch path, where admissibility is measured and from whose masks device costs may be replayed, while the A100 and H100 run the Triton path. The mask gate (Section 7) measures the mismatch, the density sensitivity (Section 5.4) bounds its effect on predictions, and admissibility transfers only if the gate passes.
- **Component profiling may misstate composed behaviour.** Null kernels can understate host cost; in-order graph replay keeps cache state between kernels but not host-induced gaps, and isolated replay (the cross-check) removes cache effects; TaxBreak notes replay is imperfect for synchronisation-heavy kernels, which describes our sparse arms. H1's error on real pairings measures how much this matters.
- **Implementations may remove the synchronisations G2 prices.** Fused, synchronisation-free estimators exist (VSPrefill). On them the model should predict no gain over simpler forms, which is a correct prediction, not a failure; the paired XAttention arms show what the synchronisations cost where they exist. Compilation with capture options may also remove some; the compiled arms measure this, and compiled variants with no wait left are predicted the same way.
- **Profitability may be empty for deployable estimators.** In the pilots none was non-inferior at any tested density. The primary claim is time prediction at a given density.
- **The dispatcher may only tie always-dense.** If no sparse configuration is admissible and faster, its value is in avoiding the kernel-speedup rule's losses, and that is the claim then.
- **A fitted baseline may win.** Each committed prediction of the fitted sum and LightGBM is trained only on the pairings banked before it: one pairing for P2, at most six for the last pairing run. The retrospective leave-one-pairing-out fits train on up to six. Either result is reported, on held-out pairings only.
- **G3 may be partly pre-empted.** Oracle-guided sparse prefill plans to measure recall of oracle support by its learned indexer. H4 is the cross-estimator test; that is how it is positioned if their follow-up appears first.
- **H4 may fail.** That is a result.
- **Cloud hosts are not controllable.** A frequency difference between sessions is the first candidate explanation for any H0 failure.
- **Two providers** add operational risk (Gate C).
- **Sparse speedups are small on the primary model.** The claims concern prediction.
- **P2's runner is rehearsed once, on an L4** (adviser's ruling 2 of the 10 Nov 2026 paste: the rehearsal's launch 4 is its last launch). Not exercised on a GPU before P2's own session: the two-path mask verdict (T2.23; the L4 has no Triton path); the A100 instance, its Flex-start and Spot provisioning, a real preemption and four Spot blocks; the lock's values, bound and learned configuration (the rehearsal runs the working ones); P2's grid above 8192 tokens and at batch 4, with its trace sizes; the compiled groups beyond 2048 at batch 1, and all of them if launch 4's compiled stage is not clean. Anything launch 4 does not reach joins this list. The list is on P2's card (`docs/launch_cards/t61_p2_a100.md`), and P2's step 1 checks every staged input a later step reads, so a missing one costs minutes. The pre-launch CPU pass (Appendix A, 11 Nov 2026) cannot run P2's or P4's own config past the start until the lock commit, so their first end-to-end pass is on the lock commit, before each card's yes.

---

## 10. Out of scope, and rejected alternatives

**Out of scope:** models needing more than one GPU; decode sparsity; natively trainable sparse attention; continuous batching and batch above 4; chunked prefill beyond Section 5.8; synthetic host speeds as evidence (delay injection only as an instrument check); a new estimator as a headline; timed kernels below block 128; cross-attention; linear attention; contexts above 32768; model families beyond Qwen2.5 and Llama-3.1; a runtime scheduler that reacts to live load.

| Rejected | Reason |
|---|---|
| A head-adaptive or entropy-driven estimator as the contribution | Already explored (FlexPrefill and Twilight, from abstract; AB-Sparse) |
| Recall by block size per head as a contribution | AB-Sparse reports it; kept as control K5 |
| Benchmarking estimator cost against quality | Method papers and CompactAttention already report it |
| Oracle masks as a quality ceiling | An attention-mass oracle is a reference, not a task optimum (oracle-guided sparse prefill; The Sparse Frontier) |
| An analytical sparse-kernel model carried across GPU generations | KernelSight-LM finds attention-kernel efficiency transfers poorly across GPU generations ("limited cross-generation transfer", its Section 8); device costs are measured |
| A fitted contention penalty | Would be tuned on the outcomes it predicts |
| A serving simulator as the baseline | Both simulators use per-device profiles and model no host work inside a step; the learned predictor and the KernelSight-style step form are fairer comparators |
| Q sensitivity over {4, 8, 16} | Far below the measured queue scale; sensitivity is relative to measured Q |
| PLA's depth-wise concentration as evidence against H4 | H4 already matches recall per layer; depth is reported as a stratum |
| A bare-metal control pairing (suggested by a reviewer) | Over budget, and the claims concern cloud hosts; the noise floor is reported instead (Section 5.6) |
| A second full-transfer pairing | Needs a new pairing; P7's host and GPU appear nowhere else, so it cannot be one. P6 stays the single full-transfer test, reported as such |
| H4 at one block size only | How recall behaves across block sizes is part of G3; the Holm family handles the extra comparisons |
| A multi-stream arm | Out of scope; the single-stream boundary is stated instead (Section 5.4) |
| Narrowing the study to G3 alone (suggested by a reviewer) | The researcher judges G2 and G3 both genuinely open (5 Oct 2026); both stay in scope |
| Dropping the fitted sum as physically unjustified (suggested by a reviewer) | Having no structure is its purpose: it is the control that shows whether structure helps |
| A runtime scheduler that routes requests by live host load (suggested by a reviewer) | Needs a serving system and a model of live load; the dispatcher decides offline per pairing, context length and batch (Section 5.9) |
| Dropping the constant-host-cost baseline (suggested by a reviewer) | Costs no runs, and tests whether per-operation host costs matter |

---

## 11. Open questions (for the professor or a reviewer)

1. Does the crossed seven-pairing design answer the validation objection?
2. Is the definition of an unmeasured pairing acceptable, and is P6's full transfer convincing?
3. Are the fitted sum (primary), sum, step form and LightGBM the right comparators; is training each committed prediction only on the pairings already run fair to the learned one, with full leave-one-pairing-out on seven pairings as the retrospective secondary?
4. Is out-of-sample time prediction enough for an IEEE Transactions journal, or is a design contribution also needed (for example a selector driven by the model's predictions)? *Researcher's answer, 6 Oct 2026: a design contribution is added, the profitability dispatcher (Section 5.9). Still open: is it enough for the target venue?*
5. If the model fails out of sample, is a well-characterised negative result publishable at the target venue?
6. Which venue: IEEE TC, TPDS, TCAD or ACM TACO; or ISPASS or MLSys first?

| `docs/code_inventory.md` | Keep / adapt / archive review of the existing code (task T0.4) |
| `docs/development.md` | Developer notes: test-suite counts and skips, and the earlier study's audit documents; its numbers are checked by `tests/test_doc_derived_numbers.py` |
| `docs/spend_ledger.md` | Cloud spend, from teardown-written `session_cost.txt` files |
| `docs/permission_log.md` | Uses of results the researcher has approved (rule 15) |
| `docs/brief/` | Source of the professor brief; `tools/build_brief_pdf.py` renders `docs/research_brief.pdf` |
| `docs/vendor_register.md` | Third-party code copied into the repository, and lines reproduced under permission (rule 8) |
| `scripts/_launch_policy.sh` | Last-launch-date and no-Australia checks used by every GCP launch script |
| `docs/limitations.md`, `docs/claims.md`, `docs/withdrawn_figures.md`, `docs/silent_failure_patterns.md` | Audit history of the banked evidence; annotate, never rewrite |
| `docs/archive/` | Superseded plans and their referee responses |
| `attnbench/` | Harness: provenance, timing, masks, backends, accuracy pipeline, analysis |
| `scripts/` | Session runners and GCP tooling |
| `tools/build_plan_pdf.py` | Renders this file to PDF |

## Appendix A. Decision record (dated, one line each)

- **4 Oct 2026.** Study direction set to predicting when sparse prefill pays across real host-GPU pairings; G2 and G3 lead, G1 is the validation question. Validation only on physically distinct pairings; synthetic host delay is never evidence.
- **4 Oct 2026.** Link cost is its own term; only device costs transfer between hosts.
- **4 Oct 2026.** Cloud CPU frequency is controlled by a measured host-speed probe with bounds fixed from P1 repeat sessions, not by frequency bands.
- **4 Oct 2026.** Baselines: sum, KernelSight-style step form, CTFusion-style LightGBM (leave-one-pairing-out). Serving simulators declined as baselines.
- **4 Oct 2026.** Budget extended by the researcher to cover the AWS pairings (P3, P5, P6, optional P7); each session still capped.
- **4 Oct 2026.** The oracle arm is an attention-mass reference, not a quality ceiling. Recall's primary reference is the arm's own forward pass; the separate dense pass is secondary.
- **4 Oct 2026.** H4 reported by depth third and by head type; recall matched per layer.
- **4 Oct 2026.** Launch-queue sensitivity is relative to measured Q (0.25x to 4x, and none).
- **4 Oct 2026.** SparKV withdrawn by its authors; not cited.
- **4 Oct 2026.** This document replaces the Estimator Frontier plan as the single source of truth.
- **4 Oct 2026.** Code inventory (`docs/code_inventory.md`) confirmed by the researcher as proposed; pre-pivot tag approved.
- **4 Oct 2026.** P1 is a development pairing; H1's confirmatory pairings are P2 to P7.
- **4 Oct 2026.** Fitted-sum baseline (a·sum h + b·sum l + c·sum d, coefficients ≥ 0, leave-one-pairing-out) added beside LightGBM as the fair test of G1.
- **4 Oct 2026.** Run order P1, P2, P4, then P3, P5, P6, P7. Last GCP launch date 22 Nov 2026, enforced in the launch scripts.
- **4 Oct 2026.** H4 sample size set before lock from a power rule; an underpowered result is reported as inconclusive.
- **4 Oct 2026.** GCP machine types and zones pinned for P1, P2 and P4 (Section 5.2).
- **4 Oct 2026.** Researcher approved using the XAttention pilot results in the brief and this plan (`docs/permission_log.md`).
- **4 Oct 2026.** The earlier Claude Doc on this project retired; it now only points here.
- **4 Oct 2026.** A100 kernel ratios restated against PyTorch's SDPA flash kernel; the "estimated A100 baselines" caveat marked unverified (T0.12).
- **5 Oct 2026.** Two referee reviews of the brief and plan triaged; eight changes adopted (the lines below). G2 and G3 both stay in scope; narrowing to G3 alone declined.
- **5 Oct 2026.** Dense baseline chosen by a fixed selection protocol (K2). Banked A100 figures restated against the fastest correct kernel, as estimates, from the caveat found for T0.12.
- **5 Oct 2026.** G2 reframed around host-visible work and synchronisations wherever masks are built; the motivating example no longer rests on the CPU reference builder.
- **5 Oct 2026.** New arm: calibrated XAttention with Python asserts removed (`python -O`), paired with the shipped arm. The arms table records where each arm builds its mask and its synchronisations per layer.
- **5 Oct 2026.** Device time measured by in-order CUDA-graph replay; isolated replay is the cross-check.
- **5 Oct 2026.** Fitted sum is the primary comparison for G1; LightGBM secondary, with a learning curve.
- **5 Oct 2026.** Recall matched only through each estimator's own budget settings; H4 bound justified at lock and a 5-point bound priced. H4 power rule written as a formula (about 1,300 × p at α 0.025 per test and 90% power; the earlier 1,080 assumed α 0.05).
- **5 Oct 2026.** Model ablations (no synchronisation term, host-only, device-only), GPU clock sampling, and p90 and p99 latency added.
- **5 Oct 2026.** G2 separated from TaxBreak in Section 3; TaxBreak's "aggregate residual" remark describes earlier work, not TaxBreak.
- **5 Oct 2026.** DFSAttn and HiSparse read in full; neither pre-empts G2 or G3. Both added to Section 3 and the brief.
- **5 Oct 2026.** A four-reviewer synthesis triaged; proposals A to L adopted (the lines below). Declined: bare-metal control, a second full-transfer pairing, H4 at one block size, a multi-stream arm (Section 10).
- **5 Oct 2026.** (A) G2 narrowed to the order and position within a prefill step of each estimator's data-dependent host work and synchronisations; KernelSight-LM's step formula and per-step scheduler cited from its full text. (L) The question split into RQ1 to RQ3.
- **5 Oct 2026.** (B) "Unmeasured pairing" defined exactly: microbenchmarks and the structural probe only; contention costed from the loaded-bandwidth microbenchmark; no trace or timing of the workload on the target is an input.
- **5 Oct 2026.** (C) Operation sequences recorded per code path, with a structural probe for new code paths; H0 and H2 compare components only within the same sequence.
- **5 Oct 2026.** (D) Gate responses fixed: replay-versus-trace bound in Gate A; null-kernel floor check before H3. (E) Single-stream scope stated.
- **5 Oct 2026.** (F) H4 confirmatory family declared (estimator pairs at three block sizes, Holm); head type and depth thirds exploratory; n sized from the upper 95% bound of the pilot disagreement rate.
- **5 Oct 2026.** (G) Constant-host-cost and nearest-measured-pairing baselines; decision error, crossover error and prediction intervals. (H) Cloud noise floor reported as a number. (I) What is claimed if sparse prefill never pays, stated in advance.
- **5 Oct 2026.** (J) Limits stated: batch 1 and 4, one stream, G3 scoped, chunked extension not representative. (K) Contention load fixed to the largest mask build per context; driver versions matched for H0; dense kernel re-checked in every timed block.
- **5 Oct 2026.** A further single review triaged; four changes adopted. (M) The two halves joined: predicted profitability on an unmeasured pairing combines the time model with the admissible density, from recall if H4 holds (T7.6). (N) Random-selection control at matched recall added to H4 and its Holm family (T1.9). (O) "One stream" explained as in-order GPU work with asynchronous host and GPU, checked from trace stream IDs; G3 stated as a test with prior evidence on both sides. (P) CPU microcode and instance generation recorded on every row (T2.20). Declined: a multi-stream model extension and a serving-simulator baseline.
- **5 Oct 2026.** Last revision from reviewer feedback before the professor's read; no further review-driven changes until then.
- **6 Oct 2026.** The researcher reopened review-driven changes for one further single review (an exception to the line above). Three changes adopted. (Q) Compiled variants of each arm under `torch.compile`, with graph breaks and remaining waits recorded (T2.21); a CPU check found that a tensor `assert`, `.item()` and `nonzero()` break the graph under default settings and a fixed-size `topk` does not. (R) A profitability dispatcher as the design contribution, answering open question 4: an offline choice of dense or sparse per pairing, context length and batch, scored by regret with the kernel-speedup rule as the primary comparator (RQ4, H6, Section 5.9, T2.22, T7.7); ground truth covers every admissible density. (S) Contributions reframed: G2 leads with the cost of data-dependent sparsity (synchronise, pad or fix sizes); TaxBreak's measurement method and the standard recursion are not claimed; scope stated for the abstract. Declined: a bare-metal control (again), a runtime load-aware scheduler, dropping the constant-host-cost baseline.
- **12 Oct 2026.** The researcher reopened changes for the methodology review of 11 Oct (an exception to the 5 Oct line) and decided the following. (1) The checks-removed XAttention arm uses a module-scoped import hook that compiles only the authors' module with asserts stripped, never the whole process; the hook neither reads nor writes the bytecode cache, and every session verifies that the default arm still carries its asserts; the arm is renamed "Python asserts removed", since Triton, `TORCH_CHECK` and other C-level checks stay. (2) Whole-process `python -O` and its dense and mean-pool controls are not run (researcher's decision; the trio was all or nothing). (3) `sys.flags.optimize` recorded on every row (K8).
- **12 Oct 2026.** (4) A synchronisation is costed once: s_0 = s_fixed (measured at the smallest payload) + payload / bandwidth, loaded where the sequence puts the copy alongside host-side work, with l_i and d_i zero for that operation; Section 5.1's c_link split so it no longer conflicts with Section 5.4. (5) Composite mechanisms (an `assert` on a GPU tensor, sizing from `nonzero()`) are split into a kernel plus a synchronisation. (6) Gate A gains a delay-injection slope check, one slope per mechanism (T2.25). (7) The replay-versus-trace check reruns on the first card of every new code path.
- **12 Oct 2026.** (8) Mask gate on P2: both XAttention code paths on one card through `use_triton`, against P1's masks; masks recorded on the target only for the first card of a new code path, and only if the gate fails (T2.23). (9) P4's Triton masks compared with P2's; if they differ, P6 records its own masks and the full-transfer claim is restated (T2.24). (10) Prediction inputs restated uniformly: target microbenchmarks plus the operation sequence and the estimator's selection as functional artifacts per code path, durations discarded; no timing or trace of the composed workload on the target. (11) Mask density and executed density on every row; predictions recomputed at plus and minus the cross-path density difference.
- **12 Oct 2026.** (12) Timed short lengths cost like any other cell. (13) Context grid 2048, 4096, 8192, 16384 and 32768 for every arm (researcher's decision: a balanced grid, since dropping 65536 removes nearly all the extra cost and the short end is where G2's contrast is largest); 2048 and 4096 are model-validation points, not operating points; 65536 dropped (Qwen2.5 stops at 32768; Llama-3.1-8B does not fit there on an L4); cost controlled on the arm-and-density cross; if lengths must be cut, the full grid stays with the synchronising arms and their controls. (14) Crossover reported as a grid bracket widened by bootstrap intervals; H3 predicts a grid interval; the quadratic fit is secondary, with its residual. (15) H6 scored at 16384 and 32768.
- **12 Oct 2026.** (16) The L4 is the accuracy GPU and the A100 the cross-card dense control, accepted on a task-score bound with the exact-match rate reported. (17) K2's correctness reference is float64, following the code, with the cross-backend fallback named per cell. (18) One limitation for the code-path mismatch (thresholds, admissibility and replayed masks from the L4 PyTorch path; Triton on the A100 and H100). (19) Primary accuracy statement is the achieved lower bound; sensitivity at 5 and 10 points; no 2-point column. (20) Margin-against-speedup frontier. (21) Primary cell n = max(200, H4 power rule) (researcher's decision: 200). (22) The union-bound test stays pre-registered; a replacement only if a simulation shows coverage, power secondary. (23) Admissibility is a decision input; its Holm family declared at lock; regret reported with and without the correction.
- **12 Oct 2026.** (24) RQ1, Section 1 and the abstract say the workload has not been timed on the target rather than not run, since the structural probe already runs each arm there at short contexts under the profiler with all durations discarded; Section 5.1 lists what may run on the target. (25) The host-cost method is credited as adapting TaxBreak's null-kernel protocol, which uses null kernels for the launch path, to the whole operation sequence, with host-side compute measured directly at target sizes; the adaptation is not claimed as a contribution. (26) The baseline is named the KernelSight-style step form in the brief as well as the plan; KernelSight-LM's Eq. 4 is named only when describing their paper, so a comparator's performance is never read as a result about their work. (27) Section 9 states what carries the isolation of the asserts-removed arm without the `-O` control: construction, verified every session (hook log, bytecode check, default-arm check).
- **18 Oct 2026.** New status word *In progress*: started, nothing usable yet, with the finished and remaining pieces named. T7.2 uses it; T0.11 becomes Partly built.
- **18 Oct 2026.** The A100 figures against the fastest correct dense kernel are stated as two kinds of number (Section 2, T0.12): the kernel ratios (0.95x at 8192, 1.84x at 16384) are recomputed from measured per-layer kernel times at the model's head layout, and the end-to-end restatements are arithmetic, not measurements. For the kernel ratios this replaces "as estimates" in the 5 Oct line.
- **18 Oct 2026.** The provisioning model (on-demand, spot, DWS flex-start or reserved) is recorded on every row (Section 5.2, T2.20).
- **18 Oct 2026.** The fitted sum and the learned predictor make committed predictions trained only on the pairings banked before each prediction, hashed with the recursion's (K9, T2.11); in the planned run order P2's are trained on P1 alone, and each prediction file records its training set. H1 scores these. Full leave-one-pairing-out fits are a labelled retrospective secondary. Gate A stays at the sum and the step form, because P1 has no other pairing to train on.
- **18 Oct 2026.** New task T0.17: GCP quota and availability for the P2 and P4 machines in their pinned zones, from the Cloud Quotas API, and whether a DWS flex-start request or the VM start counts as the launch under `scripts/_launch_policy.sh`. Code prepares the commands; the researcher runs them.
- **18 Oct 2026.** Rule 8 amended: third-party code may be copied only as listed in `docs/vendor_register.md`, each entry with its upstream, commit, licence, verbatim or adapted lines, licence and notice, reason, and approval date. Code from a repository with no licence is never vendored; its lines are reproduced only under a permission in `docs/permission_log.md`. Register drafted; approval dates and the `backends/xattention.py` entry are pending.
- **18 Oct 2026.** T0.17 also re-checks the P2 and P4 rates and records the flex-start queue wait, since the launch scripts do not pass `--request-valid-for-duration`.
- **18 Oct 2026.** New task T2.26: GPU variants of the five GQA tests, called from the session preflight, with `scripts/gpu_suite_record.sh` wired into the launch scripts. The five tests pin the CPU, so they check nothing on a GPU instance.
- **19 Oct 2026.** The reference builder's Python-overhead share is restated from about 91% to about 94% of the builder's loop time (Sections 1 and 2; researcher's decision). `docs/limitations.md` gives the profile at 32768 tokens as 0.212 s for the Python loop body and 0.013 s for the torch operations inside it, per 10 calls, so the share is (0.212 − 0.013) / 0.212 = 93.9%; no reading of those figures gives 91%. The profile ran on a laptop CPU, not on the A100 host, and was never banked, so the figure is marked unbanked until it is re-measured on the target host under T2.2. The brief is restated to match; `docs/claims.md`, `docs/limitations.md` and `docs/writeup_input.md` are annotated, not rewritten; the old figure is registered in `docs/withdrawn_figures.md`.
- **19 Oct 2026.** The method (the G2 critical-path model and the profitability dispatcher) is named BUNGA, Block-sparse Utility Navigator for GPU Attention (Section 1). The package and repository keep the name `attnbench`, the measurement harness. `README.md` was rewritten around it (T0.7).
- **19 Oct 2026.** The 6 Oct CPU compile check was reproduced and banked. A plain `assert` on a tensor does not break the graph under default settings; an assert that converts a tensor to a Python bool does. Section 1, the G2 row and T2.21 corrected.
- **19 Oct 2026.** XAttention's assert forms were read from its upstream source (commit `e379887`, `xattn/src/utils.py`; read only, nothing copied). Its prefill path makes two plain asserts on a tensor per query chunk, one inside a `try`/`except`; that form did not split the graph on a CPU. The assert joining two tensor conditions with `and`, which splits, is on the decoding path only. Both forms were added to the banked check (`results/compile_graph_breaks_cpu/`). Whether the compiled code still makes the GPU wait for them is measured under T2.21. Section 1 updated.
- **19 Oct 2026.** The H1 row adds that, if XAttention's plain asserts stay inside the compiled graph on the GPU pairings as they did in the CPU check, the compiled XAttention pair may show only a small gap, and that BUNGA's prediction of that gap, made before it is measured, is a direct test of how it prices a wait that compilation leaves in place (researcher's wording).
- **20 Oct 2026.** T0.17 done from the researcher's Mac with read-only commands: quotas, the outcome of three quota requests (preemptible CPUs raised to 26 in `asia-southeast1` and `us-central1`; GPUs across all regions kept at 1), zone availability, rates with SKU IDs (Section 5.2), the flex-start wait and when `--max-run-duration` starts counting. Facts only; the launch definition is recorded separately.
- **20 Oct 2026.** Section 5.2's rates table gains a planning rate, all-in: GPU, cores and RAM, plus the 200 GB balanced boot disk and the bundled local SSD. Session cost caps and the spend ledger use it (researcher's decision). Hours per month (730) and local-SSD partition size (375 GiB) are assumptions.
- **21 Oct 2026.** Launch definition adopted (researcher's decision, T0.17): every instance-create request, retries and the Spot fallback included, must be submitted by the end of 22 Nov 2026 IST, and a waiting request counts as launched when submitted. The A100 launcher and the L4 compile session check the date before every attempt; flex-start requests wait at most 15 min (A100) and 2 h (H100) for capacity.
- **21 Oct 2026.** XAttention is credited by authors, paper and code (Xu et al., ICML 2025; arXiv:2503.16428; `mit-han-lab/x-attention` at `e379887`, installed and called, with the lines listed in `docs/vendor_register.md` reproduced) in the README, this plan (RQ2 and the Calibrated XAttention row), `docs/limitations.md` (a dated note), `docs/t4_xattention_calibrated.md` and the brief's reference 18. `docs/permission_log.md` gains a 21 Oct 2026 entry (researcher's decision): all XAttention work, including the pilot results in `docs/claims.md` and `docs/limitations.md`, is approved by the researcher, replacing the 4 Oct 2026 scope; it applies from 21 Oct 2026.
- **21 Oct 2026.** Funding and launch window (researcher's decision): the GCP credit expires on 1 Dec 2026; ₹14,000 of new spend through 30 Nov 2026 23:59 IST; an expense after that is approved by the researcher personally and paid outside the credit; per-session caps unchanged; T3.1, T3.2, T6.1 and T6.4 priced when scheduled (Section 5.2, Funding). The last GCP launch date moves from 22 Nov to 9 Dec 2026, India time, inclusive (rule 14, `scripts/_launch_policy.sh`), and a launch after 30 Nov 2026 says it is outside the credit. Earlier Appendix A lines that name 22 Nov are left as written.
- **21 Oct 2026.** Rule 8 and the vendor register (researcher's decision): every register row states its licence status and a credit line; the five RULER rows are approved; the `xattention.py` row gains its licence status (none upstream) and its authors and paper, with its permission column unchanged. `CLAUDE.md` rule 8 now matches rule 8 here, and a new `CLAUDE.md` rule 11 says any push or merge needs the researcher's explicit yes to that exact action, naming files, branch and destination, recorded in the permission log before acting.
- **21 Oct 2026.** The frontier study's budget gate (`attnbench/analysis/frontier_prereg.py` and its pre-registration, on branch `t11-run-rates-2026-10-20`) moves to the planning rates, with the boot disk inside the rate, and its cap rises from ₹12,000 to ₹14,000 (researcher's decision; the pre-registration is an unlocked draft).
