- **21 Oct 2026.** Rule 8's last sentence (researcher's decision): code from a repository with no licence is reproduced only where `docs/vendor_register.md` lists it and the researcher's approval is recorded in `docs/permission_log.md`, replacing "only under a written permission recorded in `docs/permission_log.md`". The register's `xattention.py` row now reads "Researcher's approval recorded in docs/permission_log.md, 21 Oct 2026", pointing to that day's entry.
- **21 Oct 2026.** T0.16 carried out (researcher's decision, option 2). Branch `frontier-prereg-2026-10-03` was merged into `main` at `459c892` with a merge commit, reversing the 3 Oct 2026 "don't merge to main" for this merge only, and then pruned. Kept: the A100 and H100 baseline caveats and audit annotations, the era register (era 4 and native, with the stripped-row refusal from `a1b7801`), the per-head selector, the Llama fixes, the recall pass, splits and kernels, and T1.1's amendments A1 to A9. The split firewall and matched row budgets moved unchanged from `frontier_prereg.py` to `attnbench/analysis/splits_and_budgets.py`. Archived to `docs/archive/`: the estimator-frontier pre-registration, the rest of `frontier_prereg.py`, its plan test, the budget gate and its shell hook. The GPU launchers no longer source the gate, so the gate amendment of 21 Oct 2026 above stays on its local branch, unmerged. Not included: the README count commits and the 16 commits that exist only on the local branch (among them the vendored MInference scorer). T1.1's pre-registration gains a dated note that A6's replication link points to an archived study.
- **22 Oct 2026.** T2.8 resolutions (researcher's decision). (1) A synchronisation carries no host cost: s_fixed(m) already holds the host cost of issuing and completing it, so h_i = 0 there and the recursion refuses anything else. (2) A prefill sequence ends with an explicit closing synchronise, because the ground-truth timer synchronises before it reads the host clock. (3) The recursion without the synchronisation term is built as two named ablations: no synchronisation cost (s_0 = 0, the wait kept) and no synchronisation wait (the dependency removed). (4) The queue sensitivity rounds each multiple of Q to the nearest whole launch, never below 1, and returns the distinct depths.
- **25 Oct 2026.** T2.9 and T2.10 resolutions (researcher's decision). (1) The sum and the fitted sum stay as written; a fitted sum + s_0, labelled separately, adds the measured synchronisation cost, unfitted. (2) HDBI is sum d / (sum d + sum h), TaxBreak's Eq. 3 (arXiv:2603.12465), computed from this model's components; link costs l are outside it, and so is s_0. (3) The fitted sums minimise the plan's headline error metric, absolute relative error, over their training rows, and every fit reports a condition number. (4) The statistic predicted is the median, since Section 5.3's ground truth reports the median, p90 and p99 and names none; it is a field on every row. (5) The learned predictor keeps host_to_device_ratio, adds the sum of s_0, and computes arithmetic intensity per kernel class and transfer demand relative to link bandwidth analytically, from the model's shape and the recorded payload bytes. (6) Its learning curve runs over 1 to N - 1 training pairings for the N pairings available. (7) LightGBM is an optional extra, pinned to one version.
- **25 Oct 2026.** T1.1 scoring (researcher's decision). P-T4 is scored exactly as written in §3.9 of the archived estimator-frontier pre-registration (final text at `65b8f71`): it predicted that the `authors` table certifies 0 of 2 primary cells, and it certifies 2 of 2, so P-T4 failed. R1 to R4: not scored: comparison run not made. They compare an A100 run on the same 400 ids (the `authors` table on the Triton path, and `sdpa_flash` dense) with Session B's L4 rows; that run was not made and is not scheduled.
- **25 Oct 2026.** Phase 2 and Phase 3 dependencies, T2.10, HDBI and an L4 shakedown (researcher's decision). (1) T3.1 needs every Phase 2 task Done except T2.14's AWS half, T2.23, T2.24, T2.25 and T2.26. (2) T2.2 depends on T2.3. (3) T2.10 is Done, with the six kernel classes for arithmetic intensity and the definition of transfer demand confirmed. (4) HDBI's boundedness thresholds are fitted on the training pairings and recorded at lock. (5) A new task, T2.27: one planned L4 shakedown session, with a checklist of the L4 steps in T2.2 to T2.6, T2.13, T2.16, T2.17 and T2.21.
- **25 Oct 2026.** T2.20 and T2.7 definitions (researcher's decision). (1) A row's provisioning model is the tag that the successful create call writes into the VM's metadata, so the A100's Spot fallback is tagged Spot. It is checked against GCP's `scheduling/preemptible`; if the two disagree, or either is missing, the row records none and the reason. (2) Mask density is the arm's mask as produced, forced blocks included: today's `realised_density`, unchanged. Executed density is measured from the tensor handed to the kernel, not computed from a formula. Both count causally valid blocks times query heads. Dense rows record executed density 1.0 and no mask density. (3) T2.7 reads the Kineto trace for managed-memory use and an Nsight Systems export for page faults. A run checked only from its Kineto trace may claim no managed-memory use, not no page faults. Only the separate nsys-traced pass of the L4 shakedown may claim no page faults, and only for the arms it traced. That pass is never a timing run. It is T2.27's step 10; if nsys is not on the image, it is installed in that session.
- **25 Oct 2026.** Prediction timing (researcher's decision): the timed workload starts at least 60 s after the prediction file and its sha256 are synced, and the analysis allows 60 s of clock skew between the store's clock and the instance's (Section 5.1, T2.11).
- **25 Oct 2026.** Floors (researcher's decision): the resolution floor and the noise floor are fixed at lock from the measured run-to-run spread (Section 5.6, T4.1).
- **25 Oct 2026.** Crossover error (researcher's decision): with the same number of brackets on both sides, each predicted and measured pair is compared in order by containment of the measured bracket in the predicted one, overlap, direction of the sign change, and the shift between their centres in grid steps; otherwise the result is missed, spurious, a different count, or unresolved (Section 5.4, T2.19).
- **25 Oct 2026.** Prediction file key (researcher's decision): each committed prediction is keyed by model, code path, arm, density, context length and batch (Section 5.3, T2.11).
- **25 Oct 2026.** T2.13 records the timestamp of the first timed block, which the analysis's late-prediction check reads (researcher's decision).
- **25 Oct 2026.** New task T2.28 (researcher's decision): density sensitivity, every committed prediction recomputed at plus and minus the measured cross-path density difference, after T2.4 (Section 5.4).
- **25 Oct 2026.** T2.22 (researcher's decision): the dispatcher is no longer needed for T3.1; it becomes a prerequisite of the adversarial reads (T4.3).
- **25 Oct 2026.** HDBI fit rule (researcher's decision): one boundedness threshold, fitted on the training pairings by sign agreement, with ties going to the lower value (Section 5.4, T4.1).
- **25 Oct 2026.** `.item()` and `bool()` (researcher's decision): their payload is zero by definition, so their s_0 is s_fixed (Section 5.1).
- **25 Oct 2026.** Mechanisms (researcher's decision): a blocking host-to-device copy and an event synchronise are synchronisation mechanisms with their own s_fixed, measured under T2.5 (Section 5.1).
- **25 Oct 2026.** `cudaFree` (researcher's decision): an implicit synchronisation inside an allocator call is not costed; it becomes a trace check, and a prefill trace that shows one is flagged (Section 5.1, T2.3).
- **25 Oct 2026.** "Same sequence" (researcher's decision): a strict definition is primary and a looser one secondary, reported beside it; H0 and H2 use the strict one (Section 5.1, T2.3).
- **25 Oct 2026.** T2.5 (researcher's decision): bandwidth is measured in both directions, host to device and device to host.
- **26 Oct 2026.** T2.13 timed region (researcher's decision): every deployable arm's timed region includes its estimator, mask build, copies and synchronisations; the oracle arm may use cached scores and is labelled a non-deployable upper bound; an acceptance test, with a break-test, fails if a deployable arm's scores are computed outside the timed block (Section 5.3, T2.13).
- **26 Oct 2026.** Tie rule (researcher's decision): a predicted tie is "not profitable" (dense); sign agreement now reads it as decision error does; cells inside the resolution floor are not scored and are reported separately (Section 5.4, T2.19).
- **25 Oct 2026.** T2.6 (researcher's decision): the model's Q is the null kernel's.
- **27 Oct 2026.** Substituted positions (researcher's decision): a position the replay graph cannot hold as recorded is priced from an isolated replay of the original operator, and each position's cost source is flagged (Section 5.1, T2.4).
- **27 Oct 2026.** Chunked arms in the structural probe (researcher's decision): three probe lengths, each with at least two chunks; where the result is "different", the full-length structure is recorded on the target with every duration discarded (Section 5.1, T2.18).
- **27 Oct 2026.** The replay-versus-trace bound (researcher's decision) is set at lock from the L4 shakedown's agreement distribution (Section 7, Gate A; T2.4, T4.1).
- **27 Oct 2026.** Compiled variants run one per process in T2.13 (researcher's decision), since Dynamo does not guard compiled code on its own settings: a capture compile after a default compile of the same code reuses the default one (T2.21).
- **27 Oct 2026.** The mean-pool compiled variant is not built (researcher's decision): mean-pool builds its mask in `accuracy/model.py` (`SwappedAttention.forward`), outside any backend, so wrapping a backend in `torch.compile` does not compile its estimator, and compiling it would mean moving the estimator out of the accuracy pipeline (Section 5.5, T2.21).
- **27 Oct 2026.** XAttention kernel call (researcher's decision): `backends/xattention.py` builds the cumulative-length tensors exactly as upstream `Xattention_prefill` does at `e379887`, three device tensors from Python lists, not one shared tensor and a `torch.ones` fill. The outputs are bitwise identical (`tests/test_xattention_backend.py`); the T1.1 results carry the earlier timed-region fingerprint (`docs/vendor_register.md`; T1.1).
- **27 Oct 2026.** Oracle label (correction): "non-deployable upper bound", in the 26 Oct 2026 T2.13 line, was the researcher's own wording, and is replaced by "non-deployable reference (scoring cost outside the timed path)" in Section 5.3 step 7, the T2.13 row and `attnbench/ground_truth.py`. The 4 Oct 2026 decision stands: the oracle arm is an attention-mass reference, not a quality ceiling.
- **27 Oct 2026.** Position limit (researcher's decision): every NIAH row at 32,768 and about a fifth of `qa_1` rows there (19 to 21 of 100 per Session B file) exceed the model's positions, so the `niah_multivalue`/32768 collapse is confounded and is not a finding. Grid rule for future accuracy runs: prompt plus generation cap within 32,768 (Sections 2, 5.3 step 7 and 5.7; `docs/t4_xattention_calibrated.md`, `docs/limitations.md`).
- **27 Oct 2026.** Mask gate and H6, per-arm rule (researcher's decision, before the lock): a failed mask gate (T2.23) removes admissibility on the new code path only from the arms the gate tests, the XAttention arms; every other arm keeps the L4's admissibility, so H6 stays scorable on P2 and P4, with the removed arms named per pairing (Sections 4, 5.1 and 7).
- **27 Oct 2026.** Cells inside the floor (researcher's decision, confirming 26 Oct 2026): a cell whose measured sparse-minus-dense difference is inside the resolution floor stays unscored in decision error and sign agreement, an inadmissible cell included, and is reported separately (Section 5.4, T2.19).
- **27 Oct 2026.** Throttle reasons (researcher's decision): a timed block is flagged by hardware slowdown, software or hardware thermal slowdown, or the hardware power brake; the software power cap, normal on an L4 under load, is recorded on every row and never flags (Section 5.6, T2.13).
- **27 Oct 2026.** First-block stamp, strict rule (researcher's decision, refining the 25 Oct 2026 line): the timestamp the late-prediction check reads is the start of the first clocked block of any kind, the dense control's clocked warm-up forwards included, so a prediction synced during that warm-up is late (T2.13, T2.11).
- **27 Oct 2026.** Precomputed-mask arm (researcher's decision): batch 1 only, since `kernels.bsa_prefill`, its one kernel call, is batch 1; the batch-4 cells have no precomputed-mask arm (Section 5.3 step 7, T2.12).
- **27 Oct 2026.** FA3 (researcher's decision): an FA3 backend, for the H100 dense-baseline selection, is a prerequisite of T6.4; until one exists the H100 record is `complete: false` (Section 5.5, T2.16, T6.4).
- **27 Oct 2026.** Code-path key (researcher's decision, accepting the proposal): a code-path label is the GPU, the implementation, the optimise flag and a hash of the whole definition, without the arm; the nearest-pairing baseline matches model, arm, density, length and batch but not the code path, and the queue sensitivity pairs a sparse cell with the dense cell of its model, length and batch (T2.11, T2.15, T2.19).
- **27 Oct 2026.** Dispatcher ties (researcher's decision, accepting the proposal): ties among equal predicted times, or equal kernel speedups, go to the smaller (arm, density) (Section 5.9, T2.22).
- **27 Oct 2026.** Best-kernel comparator (researcher's decision, accepting the proposal): "always the sparse arm with the best kernel speedup" is one fixed configuration, the one with the best geometric-mean kernel speedup over the points where it is admissible, unscored where a point lacks it (Section 5.9, T2.22).
- **27 Oct 2026.** Builder profile density (researcher's decision, accepting the proposal): the reference mask builder is profiled at density 0.5 (T2.2).
- **27 Oct 2026.** Builder Python-overhead share (researcher's decision, restating the 19 Oct 2026 line): Sections 1 and 2 state it per instrument, about 94% under `cProfile` and about 53% under `torch.profiler` (lower bound 28%), from development-machine profiles at 32768 tokens, not banked. `cProfile` counts the builder's tensor element writes as Python time and `torch.profiler` counts them as operators; T2.2 banks both on the target host (Sections 1 and 2, T2.2).
- **28 Oct 2026.** FA3 build (researcher's decisions): the pin is the archived `e9515d5d` only if it has a documented successful build on this stack, otherwise the latest tagged release, never `main` (no such build is documented, so v2.8.3.post2, commit 49774b31); the build machine is `n2-standard-16`, on-demand, `us-central1`, with a hard cap sized from the planning rate; no third-party wheel (T6.4, `docs/t64_fa3_plan.md` Section 8).
- **28 Oct 2026.** FA3 restricted build (researcher's decision, after the first build was killed for memory): head dimension 128, forward, bf16 and fp16; paged KV, append KV, split KV, softcap, local attention, FP8 and backward compiled out; the flags recorded in the wheel's manifest and in the code identity; the backend refuses other head dims. Build settings: `n2-standard-16`, 15 GB per job, 1 s memory samples, 3 h cap, halt at 150 minutes, build deadline 120 minutes, and the VM halts itself when the build ends (T6.4).
- **31 Oct 2026.** Sync gap (researcher's decision, confirming the proposal of 29 Oct 2026): one source for the 60 s gap and skew (`commit.REQUIRED_SYNC_GAP_S`); every session runner waits the gap plus 30 s (`PLANNED_SYNC_WAIT_S`), and no runner or library module holds the number as a literal (a test scans for it); a start must be more than 60 s after the sync (Section 5.1, T2.11). Restates the 25 Oct 2026 line, which said "at least".
- **31 Oct 2026.** Training set (researcher's decision, confirming the proposal of 29 Oct 2026): the committed baselines' training pairings must equal the pairings banked before the prediction, in both directions; a pairing banked within the allowed skew of the write is refused as unorderable (Section 5.4, T2.11).
- **31 Oct 2026.** Scoring (researcher's decision, confirming the proposal of 29 Oct 2026): scoring reads only predictions loaded through `load_committed` with a checked sync time (K9; T2.11, T2.15).
- **31 Oct 2026.** Throttle detection (researcher's decision, confirming the proposal of 29 Oct 2026): the session's controls refuse a counters-only sampler and any flag set missing hardware slowdown, either thermal slowdown or the hardware power brake; the software power cap is recorded and cannot be chosen to flag (Section 5.3, T2.13; restates the 27 Oct 2026 line).
- **31 Oct 2026.** Ground-truth rows (researcher's decision, confirming the proposal of 29 Oct 2026): every row carries context length, batch, mask density, executed density and builder kind ("cpu" or "gpu"; none for dense), checked by `gt_schema` in the harness and the readers. The builder kind is where the row's timed mask construction runs, and the harness holds it to the probe's events; the oracle builds its mask from cached scores on the host inside the timed block, so it is "cpu" and no further value is needed (Section 5.3, T2.13, T2.20, T7.1).
- **31 Oct 2026.** K2 control (researcher's decision, confirming the proposal of 29 Oct 2026): the dense control is checked against the choice recorded for the same card, context length and batch as the prediction key (Section 5.5, T2.16).
- **31 Oct 2026.** Strict "same sequence" (researcher's decision, confirming the proposal of 29 Oct 2026): the ordered operation class, stream, blocking or not and payload class; kernel names ignored. The scored pairings per hypothesis are printed in the lock table (Section 5.1, T2.3, T4.1).
- **31 Oct 2026.** The implicit `cudaFree` wait is flagged, not refused, and the flag reaches the banked record; the blocking copy from the host and the event synchronise are no longer marked unpriced (T2.3).
- **31 Oct 2026.** Mask bank (researcher's decision, confirming the proposal of 29 Oct 2026): the manifest's density and causal fields are hashed and rechecked against the masks on load (T2.4).
- **31 Oct 2026.** T4.1 additions (researcher's decision): the list names the learned predictor's features and hyperparameters, the K2 correctness tolerance, the head-type criterion, the floors quantile, the rerun budget and the throttle set, and also the payload class edges, the prompt-set hash and tokenizer version, and the realised-length key rule. T4.1 now also depends on T1.5 and T1.3. The looser "same sequence" definition stays an open item (Section 5.1, T2.3, T4.1).
- **31 Oct 2026.** Related work (researcher's decision, applying `docs/related_work_verified.md`): KernelSight-LM's Eq. 4 and its host-constant claim are restated as the paper has them; the null-kernel comparison figures are named with their tools; The Sparse Frontier's stated unknown is quoted; MInference, FlexPrefill, SpargeAttn, CompactAttention, the SKIP paper and The Sparse Frontier have their claims verified against full text, which is not the researcher's own reading; XAttention, KernelSight-LM, TaxBreak and CTFusion stay to be read in full by the researcher; the XAttention venue is shown as arXiv, unconfirmed (Sections 3, 5.4, 5.6, 10, T7.2).
- **31 Oct 2026.** AWS (researcher's decisions of 28 Oct 2026, recorded here): last AWS launch date 20 Dec 2026, provisional; on-demand only; machine sizes as in Section 5.2; proposed planning rates recorded there; each launch's cost cap is its planning rate times its planned hours times 1.5 (Section 5.2, Section 6 rule 13, T5.1).
- **31 Oct 2026.** Plan audit (researcher's decision, applying `docs/plan_audit.md` as one batch): the edits with no open choice are applied: A1, A2, A6, B3, B5, D1, D2, D5 to D11, E1, E2, E4, E6, F1, F2, F5 and G3. Left for a decision: A3, A4, A5, A7, B1, B2, B4, B6, D3, D4, D12, E3, E5, E7, F3, F4, G4 and C4 to C8 (`docs/plan_audit.md` keeps their wording).
- **31 Oct 2026.** XAttention venue (researcher's decision, replacing the "arXiv, unconfirmed" wording of the earlier 31 Oct 2026 line): ICML 2025, Proceedings of Machine Learning Research, vol. 267, pp. 69819-69831 (<https://proceedings.mlr.press/v267/xu25ag.html>); arXiv:2503.16428 (Sections 3, 5.5, brief reference 18).
- **31 Oct 2026.** Calendar and adversarial reads (researcher's decision): the lock is by 17 Nov 2026. The first adversarial read runs during the T3.2 repeats and the second follows the T4.1 lock values; the dates in the Section 8 calendar other than the lock are planned, not decided (T4.3, T4.4).
- **31 Oct 2026.** T2.14, GCP half (researcher's decision): met by image v6 plus `scripts/gcp_bootstrap_instance.sh`; no new image is built. Each session records the image name and the bootstrap script's hash in the code identity, and the FA3 wheel is installed by its sha256 (`1676ef0b…`). The AWS half is outside the T3.1 gate and follows after 1 Dec.
- **31 Oct 2026.** T3.1 working values (researcher's decision): T3.1 is pre-lock P1 data and takes the proposal column of `docs/lock_value_table.md` for densities, ground-truth groups and repeats (with the dense rounds and the sampler interval), marked "working, pre-lock"; they are never chosen from P2 or P4 data. The floors stay null as inputs (T3.1 produces them from P1 data; the quantile is a lock value), and so does the rerun budget (decided after the T3.2 repeats). The steps read every working value from that file only. A Shakedown 2 rehearsal may use any values; its results are flagged "rehearsal" and discarded (T3.1, T4.1).
- **31 Oct 2026.** T2.6 Q probe (researcher's decision, after Shakedown 2): the warm probe, one completed launch of each kind before its blocker, is the method of record; the cold probe stays as a diagnostic (`measure_launch_queue.py --cold`). The model's Q is still the null kernel's (25 Oct 2026).
- **31 Oct 2026.** H100 FA3 gate (researcher's decision): the gate is retried every 30 minutes from the Mac (`scripts/gcp_h100_gate_retry.sh`) over the pinned zone and the asia-southeast1 fallbacks, only while no other attnbench GPU VM exists, and stops on a grant. Spot is allowed for the gate alone; P4 stays Flex-start. Cap ₹250, in-guest halt 40 minutes. A stopped or terminated request, and `ZONE_RESOURCE_POOL_EXHAUSTED`, mean no capacity: the launcher moves to the next zone and logs every attempt (T6.4).
- **31 Oct 2026.** Structural probe, dense and oracle (researcher's ruling): probe lengths are not chosen inside one cuBLAS regime, which would hide the regime changes the probe exists to detect. Library kernel selection changes the ordered operations under the strict definition, so "different" is a valid verdict, and the probe step passes when it completes with a verdict and its evidence. T2.18's fallback applies: the full structure is recorded at each grid length with durations discarded; for dense and oracle, one recording per grid length and no extrapolation (T2.18).
- **31 Oct 2026.** Memsets with no device record (researcher's ruling): tolerated, flagged and counted. Each stays an operation in the structure key, so a count that differs across lengths still reads "different". Its host cost is counted; its device cost is zero, with the cost-source flag "no device record", as for the implicit `cudaFree` wait. An upper-bound sensitivity prices those memsets at the memset zoo's measured duration for the same size and reports the shift in the prediction; the paper reports the flagged-memset count (T2.3).
- **31 Oct 2026.** XAttention probe lengths (researcher's ruling): every probe length is at or below 32768, since 34816 exceeds the model's positions and the three-length rule cannot hold as written. Three lengths at or below 32768 with distinct chunk counts of at least two where the chunk size allows; where only two exist, those two plus a full recording at 32768 as the third point. Code picks the lengths from the arm's chunk size; XAttention takes 20480, 28672 and the full recording at 32768 (T2.18).
- **31 Oct 2026.** Compiled density (researcher's ruling): "equals eager" is dropped as a gate, since a compiled run that rounds differently can threshold a slightly different mask (Shakedown 2: 0.66582 compiled against 0.66688 eager). Compiled rows take their densities from the compiled twin pass, the code path's own executed mask. Two compiled twin passes must agree exactly. The eager-versus-compiled difference is reported, flagged beyond ±0.01 absolute, a T4.1 lock value. The H6 bitwise mask gate on the compiled code path stands as measured: if the masks are not bitwise equal, the XAttention arm loses L4 admissibility on that path (T2.20).
- **1 Nov 2026.** Null pass, composite and factory calls (researcher's ruling, after the CPU dry run): the pass nulls a call at the level the dispatcher shows it, using the recorded chain of aten operators beneath each launch (`SeqOp.chain`), and a factory call (`aten::arange`) by its device argument: a launch where it names the device, host-only otherwise. A composite whose chain is not recorded is priced by the isolated replay with the cost-source flag; only where neither route reaches it is it refused. Every position priced by chain or device argument carries the flag (`chain`, `device_argument`; and `out_of_place` where the pass's own dispatch mode turns an in-place call out of place, as SDPA's math path does with its mask). The chain-priced costs of each composite must reproduce its host time measured directly in the isolated replay within a tolerance, a T4.1 lock value; a real session gates on it, a rehearsal records it (T2.2).
- **1 Nov 2026.** Replay mask check (researcher's ruling): the live masks are compared with the bank after one real replay and a synchronise. Each live mask is copied, inside the graph, into a buffer that holds a "never written" marker from before the capture, and a layer still unwritten is refused; Shakedown 3's all-False masks had read as 0.8499 agreement (T2.4).
- **1 Nov 2026.** T2.12 density (researcher's ruling): the source arm's executed density, and the precomputed arm's, are the kernel-input recorder's, which sees the mask the kernel received for any arm, as the density rule has it. A per-layer comparison with no layer on either side is refused, so no check passes or fails by comparing nothing (T2.12; also the compiled twin-pass check and the mask agreement).
- **1 Nov 2026.** Batch 4 holds dense only (researcher's default, with a check): the oracle, mean-pool and XAttention arms run at batch 1 as built. The check found the plan choosing a sparse arm per batch (Section 5.4 "Batch", H6, Section 5.9), so the narrowing is flagged to the researcher and no scope line is written until it is decided.
- **1 Nov 2026.** Batch scope narrowed (researcher's ruling on the flag above): sparse arms evaluated at batch 1; batch above 1 is future work. The confirmatory grid is batch 1, where host overhead matters most. Batch 4 keeps the dense rows; H6 is scored at batch 1; the dispatcher's choice above batch 1 is dense by construction. This narrows the generalisation the paper can claim (Sections 5.4, 5.9, H6; `docs/limitations.md`).
- **1 Nov 2026.** XAttention extension (researcher's ruling): the probe extends a chunked arm only where Ops(L) = base + chunks x body holds in every layer at every probe length, with the third point checking the fit, and the operations repeat in one periodic order; base, body and chunk counts are reported either way, and where the test fails the arm's structure is its full-length recording per grid cell (T2.18). The failure is accepted (researcher, 1 Nov 2026): on the L4 (Shakedown 4) XAttention's layer 0 has base 104 and body 72 at 3, 4 and 4 chunks (realised 20480, 28640 and 32700 tokens), predicting 320, 392 and 392 against 320, 392 and 391, one short where the last chunk's tail slice is empty (the empty `fill_`); the no-assert copy has base 107 and body 63, one short at the same length. Structures are recorded per realised length, one prompt per budget, so five per XAttention arm on P1's grid and no blow-up.
- **1 Nov 2026.** Empty calls (researcher's ruling, after Shakedown 4): a call whose outputs, or in-place target, are all empty (numel 0) launches nothing; the null pass makes no launch and gives it no position, its dispatch stays in the next launching call's gap (host cost priced, no device cost) and the profile flags that position (`empty_calls`). The CPU stand-in reads the recorded shapes to predict launches (every recorded trace now records shapes), and a GPU session checks the stand-in's prediction against its own trace and refuses on any mismatch (`cpu_boundary.launch_check`, in the recorder). Shakedown 4's L4 traces gave the stand-in two rules: four operators launch beside a preparing child (`cumsum`, `index`, `sort`, `sum`), and the two-pass oracle's mask from banked scores works on host tensors outside a host-compute region (left out of the check by name).
- **1 Nov 2026.** Kernel splits (researcher's ruling): positions are aligned to the null pass's calls by the recorded call instance (`SeqOp.calls`: the instance of every operator around each launch, the launch tied to its kernel by its correlation), never by an even split; a position with no recorded instance is refused (Shakedown 4's t22: three `addmm` calls launched 2, 2 and 1 kernels).
- **1 Nov 2026.** Compiled density (researcher's ruling): an arm that builds its own mask returns it from the compiled function (`forward_and_mask`), so the timed graph and its untimed twin are one graph; in the twin each forward starts with `torch.compiler.cudagraph_mark_step_begin()` and the mask is cloned as soon as the forward returns. Shakedown 5 measures the extra outputs' timing effect with an A/B at one length (`t228_steps.py mask_ab`); if it is material, compiled rows take their density from the eager twin, labelled "eager twin, not the executed mask", with the ±0.01 flag.
- **1 Nov 2026.** Chain validator (researcher's ruling): gated in aggregate, the summed |chain − direct| at most a working 10% of the profile's host time (T4.1), with per-call ratios reported. The ruling's other half, pricing composites by the isolated direct measurement, is held for the researcher: on Shakedown 4's banked cells leaf calls show the same chain/direct ratio as composites (0.77 to 0.80 against 0.73 to 0.85), and the recorded prefill's own host time per call is about half the null pass's (pass/recorded 2.1 to 2.5 for leaves), so the direct measurement does not isolate a composite's dispatch, and both instruments carry their own overhead (about 49 µs per launching call from the pass's two Python modes, measured on the Mac). `index_put_`'s 2.0 to 2.6 was the tape's stand-in for a boolean assignment (`nonzero_static`, `unbind`, a device fill, run for real in the pass), not a wait: the pass now runs the assignment as written and its dispatch mode nulls the `index_put_` (`TapeReplayer(serve_setitem=False)`).
- **1 Nov 2026.** Compiled XAttention's kernel interface (researcher's ruling, after Shakedown 5): it runs outside Dynamo (`torch.compiler.disable` in `forward_and_mask`), since traced, its own break left a resume frame that recompiled per call and reached the recompile limit at about the ninth prefill. Each compiled variant's declared breaks, by backend and setting (`compiled.DECLARED_BREAKS`: XAttention's kernel interface under both settings; mean-pool's two library breaks under the default settings, as the L4 reported them on 31 Oct 2026), are its code-path identity: matched by reason, file and function in every layer, never by line, and a report with an undeclared or a missing break is another code path and is refused. A recompile-count gate refuses a compiled arm that compiles after its warm-up (working 0, T4.1). Rows carry the count and the identity (T2.21).
- **1 Nov 2026.** Mask-output A/B (researcher's ruling, after Shakedown 5): compiled XAttention keeps its mask as an output of the compiled function and compiled rows take the executed mask's density. The A/B measured B − A = −11.5 ms (−1.5%) at 8075 tokens, one length, one session, one side per process (A, B, B, A), with the kernel interface outside Dynamo; it is recorded as a limitation, not a correction (`docs/limitations.md`).
- **1 Nov 2026.** Host costs for predictions (researcher's ruling, after Shakedown 5, revising the 31 Oct 2026 design in which the prediction's host costs were measured under the observer, `delay_injector.observed`): the prediction reads uninstrumented host costs, the bare timer's. Every call the null pass meets, a leaf or a composite, is re-issued alone at its prefill inputs at least 1000 times, each after a synchronise outside the timer, and priced at the median less the timer's own overhead; the instrumented/bare ratio is recorded per call, per operator and per cell. The null pass is kept as the instrumented comparison; host-compute regions keep its direct time and the isolated replay's positions keep theirs, labelled (T2.2, T2.25).
- **1 Nov 2026.** Chain gate in the zoo, oracle re-evaluation and fallback labelling (researcher's ruling, after Shakedown 5): the aggregate gate (T4.1 row 32, working 10%) compares each composite's chain, the summed bare times of the children that launched its positions, with its own bare time; a call that launches its own kernel beside its children has no such chain and is priced as a leaf. A cell that fails is not refused: its composites take their own bare time, and the cell and its prediction carry the label "chain gate failed: composites priced by their own bare time, not by their leaves". The oracle, which failed the instrumented check on Shakedown 4's cells (22 to 26%), is re-evaluated under this rule in Shakedown 6. The instrumented check (null pass against the isolated replay) is recorded, never gated (T2.2).
- **1 Nov 2026.** H100 Spot cap (researcher's rule, after Shakedown 5): Spot's hard delete is at least 40 minutes after RUNNING and its cap at most ₹350. At the Spot planning rates (₹629.94/h and ₹668.04/h, Section 5.2) 40 minutes cost ₹420 and ₹446, so the gate loop refuses Spot until a rate of ₹525/h or less is on record (T6.4).
- **1 Nov 2026.** The kernel interface outside Dynamo for every arm (researcher's ruling, after Shakedown 6, where compiled mean-pool recompiled per call under the default settings at the library's `replace_ones_with_count`, XAttention's Shakedown 5 defect): the oracle, mean-pool and XAttention call the block-sparse kernel through one function, `block_sparse.kernel_interface`, which Dynamo calls outside itself (`torch.compiler.disable`) and an eager run calls directly. Each compiled variant of a block-sparse backend now declares that one break under both settings (T2.21's code-path identity), and the recompile gate (T4.1 item 33) holds it. The mean-pool backend's `try` around the kernel moved inside the excluded call, since a break inside a `try` is a break of its own.
- **1 Nov 2026.** Once-only host pricing (researcher's ruling, after Shakedown 6, where XAttention's null pass was refused: the recording nests `aten::scalar_tensor` inside `aten::where`, which the dispatcher shows as two calls): each launching call the pass makes is priced once, as part of the recorded call that nests it. Calls to operators in the call's chains may come before its own operator, at most one fewer than its positions; each takes its positions in order. The bare timer aligns the same way. Tested on Shakedown 6's own sequence as a fixture, with a conservation test: every pass call used once, and the positions' costs summing to the pass's calls plus one floor per further kernel. The CPU stand-in now gives `scalar_tensor` its launch, as the L4 does.
- **1 Nov 2026.** The per-layer residual (researcher's ruling, after Shakedown 6, on the bare prices leaving out the arm's own Python between calls): per layer, the prefill's host span under the tape alone, with real kernels and two clock reads per layer (a boolean-mask write timed after a synchronise), less the layer's bare prices. It is added to the layer's call positions in proportion to the null pass's gaps. A negative residual adds nothing and is labelled. Its spread over the timed repetitions (the interquartile range over the median) has a T4.1 tolerance (item 34, working 10%); a layer above it, or one where the device fell behind the host, keeps its residual and labels the cell. It is not refused.
- **1 Nov 2026.** H100 Spot cap and the gate's expected duration (researcher's ruling, after Shakedown 6, revising the line above, which refused Spot at both rates): the Spot cap (at most ₹350) is held by the in-guest halt, as on every other card. The halt comes at the minutes ₹350 buys at the Spot rate, never after the hard delete (at least 40 minutes). Spot is refused when those minutes are fewer than the gate's expected duration, 30 minutes from boot to the gate's own halt step (the T6.4 card's 20 minutes of setup and 10 of steps; Shakedown 6's L4 took 5.6 minutes from launch to its first step), never taken lower. At ₹668.04/h and ₹629.94/h the halt is at 31 and 33 minutes (₹346 and ₹347). The delete at 40 minutes bills ₹446 or ₹420 only if the halt fails.
- **2 Nov 2026.** Block-sparse kernel boundary read (researcher's ruling, after the XAttention crash diagnostic). At the pinned Block-Sparse-Attention commit (`49d6c39`) the forward kernel reads one entry past any fully selected row of its int32 row mask, and for the last row of the last head one entry past the buffer. In the source the value read is discarded, and the read faults only where the address is unmapped. That was T3.1's first session (`docs/t31_xa_diag_card.md`). The adapter, not the library, now hands the kernel a buffer with a row of -1 after it (`attnbench/bsa_contract.py`, fix `row-mask-tail-1`). Every row from now records the library's version, its interface file's sha256 and the fix, and each block-sparse arm's code identity includes them. T3.1's preflight runs a kernel-contract check and refuses the session on any failure. The check covers the pinned library; the kernel against a reference masked attention on masks with every head's last row fully selected; any output difference made by the value past a row or by the padding; and memcheck with and without the fix. Results banked before the fix ran the library's own buffer (`docs/limitations.md`). XAttention reached the read in every banked prefill. A small T1.1 `qa_1` re-check is queued after T3.1, in a gap between L4 sessions. T3.1 relaunches directly with this preflight, and the 4 Nov quarantine fallback stands.
- **2 Nov 2026.** The `qa_1` re-check (researcher's ruling, after Code's reading of the kernel source: the value read past a row is discarded, so the effect is expected to be crash-only). It is dropped if T3.1's step 1 is clean: the library's own buffer and the fixed one give bitwise-identical outputs on every synthetic mask, those with every head's last row fully selected included, and on a real XAttention prompt (the grid's first 2048 prompt, every layer's output). An Appendix A line then records the effect as crash-only, and the limitations note takes the measured result. A difference refuses the session in step 1 and keeps the re-check. The fix is required for every XAttention run from now on.
- **2 Nov 2026.** The block-sparse kernel's boundary read is crash-only, measured (researcher's rule of 2 Nov 2026, line above). In T3.1's second session, step 1 compared the library's own buffer with the fixed one (`results/t31_p1_s2`). Outputs were bitwise identical on the synthetic masks at 1951 and 4096 tokens, every head's last row fully selected included, and on all 28 layers of XAttention's real `qa_1` prompt at 1951 tokens. The `qa_1` re-check is dropped, and results banked before the fix stand. The session stopped at the same step: memcheck saw no error with the library's own buffer, so the check could not see the read, which likely landed in a neighbouring allocation. memcheck now pads every allocation (`--padding`).
- **2 Nov 2026.** T3.1's step 5 memory (researcher's ruling, after the third session, whose step 5 the out-of-memory killer took at 31.6 GB on `g2-standard-8` in `oracle_d0.5_L32768`). Measured on the banked cells, both causes held: memory built up across cells in the one process, and that cell's isolated replay was large on its own. The fix: each cell runs in its own process under an RSS ceiling (26 GiB) that stops the cell, not the step, with its record. The isolated replay's trace is read as a stream and kept as a compact event stream, without arguments or device activity, and read once. Each cell's manifest records its peak RSS. A cell resumes only under the same commit, clean tree, libraries and instance. The recording's trace and its profiler settings are unchanged: the sequence parser reads its Python frames and the launch check its shapes. A larger machine was refused: P1's host is pinned (Section 5.2). The next session starts from step 1 and reruns all of step 5: none of the third session's 31 cells is reused, because process isolation changes warm-up a little and T3.1 feeds the floors and Gate A, so its rows come from one code identity.
- **2 Nov 2026.** T3.1's step 6 memory (researcher's answer, after the step-5 fix, asked to launch: fix step 6 first). Step 6 runs each cell in its own process under the same ceiling, with the same manifests and resume, and its cells' inputs (the host cell's entry and the link row) pinned by sha256, so a resumed device cell is one priced from the same host step. Its one shared measurement, the memset zoo, is taken once and kept for the step's later cells. The isolated replay of the original operators is not compacted: the replay's matching reads its device events and correlations. The projection adds a process start per cell to step 6: 4.0 / 4.8 / 6.3 hours, all within the ₹760 cap. Found while checking resume on the CPU: the session runner moved every step's directory aside on `--from`, so no cell of step 5 could ever resume. A resume now leaves steps 5 and 6 in place for their cells and keeps the earlier attempt beside them as a hard-linked copy.
- **2 Nov 2026.** T3.1's fourth session stopped at `oracle_d0.5_L32768` a second time (researcher's rulings after it). A debug session (`scripts/t31_debug.py`, `docs/t31_debug_card.md`) stages that session's passed step-5 cells from the bucket and runs steps 5 or 6 to 11 once with `--keep-going`, to find every failure in steps 6 to 11 without paying for step 5 again; the cell is not measured in it, every output is labelled "debug, not T3.1 data", its prediction goes under `predictions/P1-debug`, and it is capped at 1.5 times the projected steps. Its data is discarded; the T3.1 session then runs end to end on the fixed code. A `not_measured` list in a session's config removes cells from the grid for every step, which is also how `oracle_d0.5_L32768` is dropped for all pairings ("not measured: host memory limit of P1's pinned machine") if the segmented isolated replay is not validated by 5 Nov.
- **2 Nov 2026.** The isolated replay runs in blocks of two decoder layers (researcher's ruling 2, after T3.1's fourth session): a profiler session per block, rolled between operators, each block's compact stream joined by concatenation into the one the readers take. The fourth session measured the profiler at five times its trace (15.7 GiB at `oracle_d0.25_L32768`, killed at the 26 GiB ceiling at density 0.5). Checked offline: the 31 banked streams cut into 14 pieces and stitched read as the originals (31 of 31 exact), and the roll on a CPU. Not checked: a CUDA profiler's memory on the card; the T3.1 session's own manifest for that cell is the first number. Step 6's full reads of the original-operator replay and the recording's trace at that cell are not covered. If the segmentation is not accepted by 5 Nov the cell is dropped from the grid for every pairing.
- **7 Nov 2026.** P4 may run on Spot (researcher's ruling, after the first H100 gate): the row records `spot`, and a session whose instance was interrupted (preempted and started again) is discarded in full, with no resume and none of its rows used as evidence. `scripts/t61_session.py` records the boot id in the session's manifest and, for the T6.4 runners, refuses a resume on another boot and writes `INTERRUPTED_DISCARD.md`; a step-level `--from` on the same boot is unchanged. The gate was already allowed Spot (31 Oct 2026). Not yet on the P4 launch card: the Spot rate, cap and halt for a timed session.
- **7 Nov 2026.** The FA3 gate checks exact only (researcher's ruling, after the first H100 gate): in a gate-only run a configuration whose float64 oracle does not fit is left out and listed, never checked against other backends, because FA3 alone has none to compare with (6 Nov 2026: two batch-16 configs failed that way with no error measured, while all 25 exact checks passed). The model's head layout (12:2, batch 1, causal) is added at 8192, 16384 and 32768 tokens against a float64 reference computed a block of queries at a time (`gates.blockwise_reference`, the oracle's own arithmetic without the S x S matrix). Run-phase syncs leave out the `results/` directories the repository tracks.
- **7 Nov 2026.** The H100 FA3 correctness gate passed (second attempt, 6 Nov 2026 21:29Z, Spot, 10 minutes, ₹111): the T2.16 gate on the H100 is met. P4's timed session on Spot is planned as short blocks (researcher's ruling, 7 Nov): about two hours or less each, the prediction committed once and reused by every block, each block's cap the Spot rate x its planned hours x 1.3 and its halt 1.5 x those hours; the numbers are computed from the P4 launch card by `scripts/t64_blocks.py`. A block-aware runner (ground truth split across blocks) is not built.
- **7 Nov 2026.** The link microbenchmark measures every mechanism the recursion prices (researcher's ruling 2 of 6 Nov, after the T3.1 debug session's `predict` refused `copy_from_host_pageable`): `link.measure` times the five synchronising mechanisms and the three Appendix A (25 Oct 2026) added, an event synchronise and a blocking copy from pinned and from pageable host memory, with their payload term for the four blocking copies (host to device as well as device to host), over 4 KiB to 256 MiB. A completeness test ties the measured list to `recursion.PRICED_MECHANISMS`. The prediction keeps refusing a priced mechanism with no measurement and a blocking copy above the largest measured payload; it never defaults or extrapolates. T3.1's step 2 gains about three minutes (not measured on a GPU).
- **7 Nov 2026.** Step 6 prices by structure (researcher's ruling 1 of 6 Nov 2026, after the T3.1 debug session found the name rule refusing every sparse cell: a CUDA graph runs a device copy as `memcpy32_post`, a copy may run another launch variant, and an eager run adds host-device copies). `replay.match` pairs replayed nodes with positions by activity class (a graph's copy kernel is the device copy it runs), stream by order of first use, order and payload class; names only place the comparison, and a stretch between two name-matched blocks is paired only if it agrees in structure one for one, so a stand-in of another kernel count (`nonzero_static` for `nonzero`) is never priced as the operator it replaces. A one-kernel stand-in for one kernel is matched as a variant, which structure alone cannot tell apart. Every priced position is labelled (`graph`, `isolated_original`, `variant_differs`), the nodes dropped are counted (`extra_node_dropped`), and per arm over its cells the share of priced device time priced from the isolated original must be at most a T4.1 lock value, the pricing mismatch share (row 35, working 5%); an arm above it, or with a device position neither replay matches, is not priced, is labelled "pricing not validated" in step 6's `pricing_gate.json` and the committed prediction's `not_priced`, and is kept out of the hypothesis claims; the bound is never widened to pass. Step 7 reports each arm's share of prediction time priced from the isolated replay (`isolated_share.json`, beside the committed file). The debug session's banked 2048-token cells: every position priced; shares 0% (dense), 0.17% (oracle), 0.20% (mean-pool), 0.97% and 0.99% (the two XAttention arms).
- **7 Nov 2026.** Step 6's isolated replay of the original operators is written as a compact event stream (researcher's ruling 3 of 6 Nov 2026): replayed in blocks of two layers like step 5's, each block's trace reduced to the region, the flushes, the launching calls and the device activities with their correlation, stream and bytes, the blocks stitched with their correlations offset, and read lazily by `replay.replay_nodes` in three passes. Validated offline on session 3's banked 3.29 GB `oracle_d0.25_L32768` trace (the same replay of the original operators, unsegmented): compaction 42.8 s at a peak RSS of 0.27 GB (4.80 million of 14.03 million events kept, 646 MB), the lazy read and the structural match 23.2 s at 0.33 GB, all 2,092 device positions matched and the 84 extra nodes all host-device copies; the debug session's full read of the same kind of file was killed above 26 GiB. The segmented replay's own peak on the GPU host is still unmeasured. `oracle_d0.25_L32768` is kept; the drop fallback for `oracle_d0.5_L32768` stays in force (the 5 Nov deadline was missed).
- **7 Nov 2026.** The teardown keeps the bulk results in the bucket (researcher's ruling, 6 Nov 2026, after the debug session's teardown copied hard-linked attempt directories as 25 GB and more onto a Mac with 33 GiB free): `gcp_teardown_session.sh` has the instance sync once more with the phases' own sync (`run_phase.sh --sync-only`), then pulls only the logs and manifests (every `*.log`, manifest, step result, receipt, cell exit record and discard note under `results/` and `logs/`) as one archive, and checks before every copy that the local disk holds twice its size plus a reserve (default 2 GB). A sync that fails while SSH works refuses the delete unless `TEARDOWN_DELETE_WITHOUT_BUCKET_SYNC` names the reason; with SSH gone the home-logs override covers it. The AWS teardown (`aws_teardown_session.sh`) still copies `results/` whole; P5 has no bucket sync yet.
- **7 Nov 2026.** The next T3.1 session is exploratory (researcher's ruling 4 of 6 Nov 2026): T3.1 live from step 1 (`t31_debug.py explore`, `t31_session.py --debug --keep-going`), every output labelled "T3.1 exploratory, code identity may change", its prediction under `predictions/P1-exploratory`, a fix resumed on the instance with each step's passed cells resumed under the session's own earlier commits, and a failure in steps 1 to 5 blocking every step after it but the postflight. Cap ₹500: no step starts after 230 minutes, the in-guest halt at 265 minutes and the hard delete 10 minutes after it (₹498 at ₹108.64/h). T3.2a and T3.2b then run on the final code identity as the data of record; repeatability, the Gate A inputs and the floors come from them.
- **7 Nov 2026.** Spot cards (researcher's ruling, replacing the halt of 1.5 x the planned hours, which cost 1.15 x the cap): per block, the cap is the Spot rate x the planned hours x 1.3, the in-guest halt 1.3 x the planned hours rounded down to the minute, so the cost at the halt is never above the cap (`scripts/t64_blocks.py` refuses a block where it would be, and a test holds it at every block length), and the hard delete 10 minutes after the halt. The H100 FA3 gate, already passed, kept its own Spot terms (a cap of at most ₹350 held by the halt).
- **7 Nov 2026.** A block-aware runner (researcher's ruling, 7 Nov 2026): the block spec is in the lock file (`blocks.<pairing>`, `attnbench/lock/blocks.py`), and `scripts/t61_session.py run --lock FILE --block ID` runs one block as its own session, on its own boot: the boot steps (`prereqs`, `preflight`, the end host-speed probe; P4 adds `fa3_import`) and the block's own steps. Every other step is in exactly one block, in the session's order; `predict` is in one block with ground truth after it, and every later block reuses that prediction; ground truth is split across blocks by cell, no cell in two blocks, and one anchor cell, in no block's own cells, is measured in every block with ground truth. A block imports every earlier block's session directory (`--import-block`), checked before anything runs (the same spec and code commit, every step passed, not discarded), and after its preflight its instance type, GPU and CPU model must be the earlier blocks'. Every ground-truth row carries its block's id and whether it is the anchor. An interrupted block writes `INTERRUPTED_DISCARD.md` and is discarded alone, for P2 as for P4. The analysis reads the blocks (`attnbench/analysis/block_drift.py`), sets a discarded block aside, refuses blocks of two specs or commits, and compares the anchor between blocks against a bound passed in; the bound is not yet a lock value. For P4, 3 blocks of at most 2.2 h; P2 uses the same runner. Tested in dry runs; the real ground-truth harness and several component steps of the P2 and P4 runner are still not built.
- **7 Nov 2026.** The T3.1 exploratory session ran (researcher's ruling 4 of 6 Nov 2026; L4, ₹326, 180 min, not T3.1 data): steps 1 to 8 passed on the whole grid less `oracle_d0.5_L32768`; step 6's pricing gate validated dense (0%), mean-pool (0.06%), XAttention (1.28%) and XAttention without asserts (1.28%) against the working 5% and refused the oracle, whose 16 unpriced positions (cells L8075 at density 0.5, L16370 and L32700 at 0.25) were the `masked_fill_` of one layer and the three `nonzero` kernels of the next; in step 9 the probe first missed `XAttentionNoAssertBackend.estimate` (fixed and resumed), then the eager group passed at every length and `compiled_default` reached Dynamo's recompile limit, the probe's wrapper having been traced inside compiled XAttention's `forward_and_mask`.
- **7 Nov 2026.** Compiled arms (researcher's ruling 1 of 7 Nov 2026): the ground truth's probe patches nothing inside an arm's compiled region (`CompiledBackend.compiled_region_targets`). A compiled arm whose estimator runs inside its compiled function logs no score event, and two untimed checks after its timed blocks and its recompile gate stand in for them (`attnbench/compiled_checks.py`): the Kineto twin check (per layer, the kernels launched inside the layer's compiled call and outside the kernel interface, at least one and the same in a plain recording of the compiled path and in its twin pass) and the two-prompt mask dependence check (the compiled masks change between the cell's prompt and a second of the same length, by the eager masks' change within ±0.01, the compiled-versus-eager tolerance); a cell without both passed is refused. Built and tested on a CPU with Dynamo's eager backend; not run on a GPU.
- **7 Nov 2026.** The oracle's unpriced positions (researcher's ruling 2 of 7 Nov 2026): the isolated replay already rolled its profiler in each decoder layer's forward pre-hook, the hook that labels the recording's layers, and now refuses a roll that is not at its layer boundary. On session 3's recordings at the same lengths, a match of the whole stream reports a loss of 60 to 79 nodes straddling a layer boundary, anywhere in the prefill, as exactly the pattern seen at the last layers; the pulled manifests carry no segment bounds. Step 6 now matches the isolated original segment by segment (`replay.match_segmented`: each segment's positions against its own profiler session's nodes, told by their correlations), and its result names each segment with a loss, with its launches that no device activity answered. The oracle's step-6 cells are rerun on the exploratory session's step 5 before T3.2a; only if the oracle still fails the gate is the fallback built: imputation, flagged, at most 1% of the arm's priced device time.
- **7 Nov 2026.** The block drift bound (researcher's ruling 3 of 7 Nov 2026) is a T4.1 lock value, working 3%: a pairing run in blocks compares the anchor cell's untraced median in each block with the first block's, and a block above the bound is named, its rows are labelled "drift above bound" and kept out of the hypothesis claims; it is never widened to pass (`attnbench/analysis/block_drift.py`).
- **7 Nov 2026.** T3.2a (researcher's ruling 4 of 7 Nov 2026): the eager ground-truth group only, real data, its code identity frozen at the commit of its first start (`t31_session.py --repeat a --groups eager` refuses a run at any other commit, so no fix is made within the session), its cap from the exploratory session's measured durations: central 213 minutes, in-guest halt 277 minutes (1.3 x central), hard delete 287 minutes, ₹520 (`docs/t32a_card.md`).
- **8 Nov 2026.** T3.2a ran (L4, ₹411, 227 min; `results/t32a_p1_20261107T1640Z`, frozen at `3f50aab`): phase 0 reran the exploratory session's step 6 on its nine oracle cells with the segment match, and the oracle validated (0 positions unpriced, 16 before; share 0.05%); then steps 1 to 11 passed on the eager group, step 6's pricing gate validating every arm (dense 0%, oracle 0.05%, mean-pool 0.05%, XAttention 1.24%, XAttention without asserts 1.23%), the prediction synced 228 s before the first timed block. A first phase-0 attempt stopped at the wrapper's own check (it counted ten oracle cells; the exploratory session has nine, `oracle_d0.5_L32768` being the drop fallback).
- **8 Nov 2026.** Memsets with no device record at 32768 tokens (T3.2a's bucket artifacts, read offline): every 32768-token cell, in every arm, has 84 `cudaMemsetAsync` launches that no device activity answers, three per layer, each inside `aten::linear > aten::addmm` (the q, k and v projections, which carry a bias) just before the projection's GEMM. Step 5's eager recording has them too (the dense cell included, which has no isolated replay), and step 6's isolated replay shows them six per two-layer segment in all 14 segments; they are the same positions every time, so they are not records lost at random. At 16384 tokens the same projections issue two memsets per layer, each with a 1024-byte device record; at 16384 x 4 one per layer, with none. The trace gives no size for a memset without a device record, so whether they are zero-length is not known. They are T2.3's flagged memsets (532 in T3.2a's prediction: 6 x 84 + 28): the primary prediction adds nothing for them, and the sensitivity bound adds the memset zoo's largest duration (4.22 µs) to each one's `addmm`, 0.35 ms per 32768-token cell (`memset_max_shift_s`); none is an unpriced position.
- **8 Nov 2026.** T3.2a's `oracle_d0.5_L8192` loss sits at a segment's start: the three positions the isolated replay's segment match left unmatched (2013 to 2015, layer 26's input RMSNorm: `_to_copy`, `pow`, `mean`) are the first three kernel launches of the last segment's profiler session (layers 26 and 27), and they are the segment's three launches with no device activity. The cut itself is at the layer boundary (`_Rolled.check_cuts`); the device records of the first launches after the profiler restarted were not written. The graph replay priced all three, so the oracle's pricing is unaffected (0 unpriced); no other segment of T3.2a lost a record.
- **8 Nov 2026.** `oracle_d0.5_L32768` is not measured on any pairing (the drop fallback of 2 Nov 2026, kept by ruling 3 of 6 Nov 2026): T3.2a ran without it, as the exploratory session did, and T3.2b will. The segment match now prices the oracle's other cells, 32768 at density 0.25 included, but this cell was never run under it; its step-5 memory at 31.6 GB on P1's pinned host stays the reason. Every T3.1 and T3.2 session config since 2 Nov 2026 names it in `not_measured` ("not measured: host memory limit of P1's pinned machine"; `t31_debug.STUCK_CELL`, which `t31_session.repeat_config` adds), so the oracle's rows at 32768 tokens have density 0.25 only; the base config `configs/t31/p1_session.json` names it too since 8 Nov 2026 (below), and the P2 and P4 grids are fixed in the lock, whose ground-truth grid (T4.1) is to record the omission for every pairing (`docs/limitations.md`).
- **8 Nov 2026.** The analysis reads a committed prediction on any Python (found in T3.2a's offline read): `commit.verify_bytes` recomputes the sum, the sum with s_0 and the step form from the committed components and compares exactly, and those used Python's `sum`, which Python 3.12 made compensated for floats. The instance runs Python 3.10 and the Mac 3.12, so the Mac refused T3.2a's file (96 values off by up to 1.2e-14 relative). The baselines now add left to right on every version (`baselines.plain_sum`), the order 3.10's `sum` uses, which reproduces every committed T3.2a value exactly; the check stays exact. The session's code is unchanged: T3.2b runs `3f50aab`.
- **8 Nov 2026.** Gate A tooling (T3.3): `attnbench/analysis/gate_a.py` and `scripts/gate_a.py` read one session's banked files and report Gate A's three legs, the decision staying the researcher's. Leg 1: per predictor, overall and per arm class, the median absolute relative error of the recursion, the sum and the step form, read through `load_committed`. Leg 2: per operation family, the device time the prediction priced at each position against step 5's traced recording, with the spread over cells, at a bound the run names (a lock value) or none. Leg 3: the delay-injection slopes, reported as not measured when the session ran none. T3.1's steps include no delay injection (T2.25 is built and tested on CPU only), so neither T3.2 repeat measures leg 3, and Gate A cannot be read until a session runs it. The dry run on T3.2a alone is `docs/t32a_gate_a_dry_run.md`, labelled one repeat, not the decision.

T3.1's steps find the runner's directories, numbered in its own order and copied in for an imported block, through the session's `step_layout.json`. P2's and P4's values are fixed at lock (T4.1): until then T3.1's guard refuses to fill them with P1's working values, so these steps refuse with that reason, not "not built". Still not built: the mask gate and mask recording (T2.23), the cell assembler (`predict`'s record, with the fitted baselines trained on P1) and the ground-truth grid driver (untraced then traced, per block). Not run on an A100 or an H100.
- **8 Nov 2026.** T3.2b ran (L4, `3f50aab`, `results/t32b_p1_20261107T2100Z`): steps 1 to 11 passed on the eager group, step 6's pricing gate validating every arm (dense 0%, oracle 0.05%, mean-pool 0.06%, XAttention 1.27%, XAttention without asserts 1.27%), the prediction synced 157 s before the first timed block. 248 minutes billed, ₹449 (`docs/spend_ledger.md`); the logs and manifests were pulled from the bucket, where every step had synced.
- **8 Nov 2026.** P1's training set for the fitted baselines (researcher's decision, for P2's committed prediction and every later one trained on P1): **one row per cell, its target the median of both T3.2 repeats' samples pooled**; each repeat's median is kept with the row as metadata, so the between-session spread is reported. Pre-declared sensitivity analyses, reported and never used to choose the primary: the two repeats as separate rows, and repeat a alone. Cross-validation groups by cell. Exploratory and debug sessions never enter training. Had T3.2b failed, the pooled row would be repeat a alone.
- **8 Nov 2026.** Offline checks on both T3.2 repeats (researcher's item 2 of 8 Nov 2026, second paste; bucket artifacts, read on the Mac). (a) The record-less memsets at 32768 tokens (84 per cell, three per layer; also 28 per cell at 16384 x 4, the q projection) are zero-length, not dropped records: such a call costs 0.54 µs of CPU at the median against about 9 µs for one that enqueues work; no other kind of launch lacks a device record; and the device timeline has no room for them (a memset that runs occupies at least 2.62 µs between its neighbours, over 5,656; the gap where these would sit is at most 1.95 µs, over 1,064). Device time at the headline length is not understated. (b) The isolated-replay losses after a profiler restart are three segments in 812, each cut at the layer boundary: T3.2a's `oracle_d0.5_L8192` (layer 26, three launches) and T3.2b's `oracle_d0.5_L8192` (layer 12, one) and `oracle_d0.25_L16384` (layer 26, seven), each a run of the layer's input RMSNorm, all priced by the graph replay. (c) `oracle_d0.5_L32768` is now in `configs/t31/p1_session.json`'s `not_measured`, with the P2 and P4 configs' wording; the Appendix A line above and `docs/limitations.md` already recorded it. (d) The same-session per-arm numbers are in `docs/t32_gate_a_repeats.md`, labelled descriptive.
- **8 Nov 2026.** Gate A over both repeats (researcher's item 3 of 8 Nov 2026, second paste): `attnbench/analysis/gate_a_repeats.py` and `scripts/gate_a_repeats.py` read each session with `gate_a.report`, against its own ground truth, and compute the floors from both with `lock.floors`. The plan's thresholds are applied as written and none is widened: leg 1 holds only if the recursion's median absolute relative error is below the sum's and the step form's over every cell in every session; leg 2 only at a lock bound with no cell outside it; leg 3 only with every slope measured within a lock bound; a leg that cannot be applied does not pass. On T3.2a and T3.2b: leg 1 fails in both (recursion 13.8% and 14.5%, step form 3.8% and 3.6%, sum 96.8% and 98.5%), legs 2 and 3 are not applied, so Gate A does not pass from these data as written; the decision stays the researcher's (T3.3). Floors at the provisional quantile 0.95: noise floor 6.77%, resolution floor 19.42 ms (`docs/t32_gate_a_repeats.md`, with the per-cell between-session spread).
- **8 Nov 2026.** Every P2 and P4 session ends itself (researcher's item 4 of 8 Nov 2026, second paste): `scripts/session_exit.sh` runs the session and, on any exit (passed, failed, refused, killed, hung up on), syncs its logs, manifests and every `~/*.log` to the bucket and then halts the VM, with no watcher on the Mac; `t61_session.py` refuses a real start outside it. The teardown pulls the logs and manifests from the bucket when the instance is halted or gone (step 2c), and its exit marker stands in for the last sync and the home logs. - **8 Nov 2026.** The revised code identity (adviser's ruling B of 8 Nov 2026, fourth paste), final for P2 and P4, every method below declared before any data under it. (1) **The per-layer residual is measured with the device idle** (`layer_residual.py`, schema `/2`): the host gaps between the prefill's calls, under the tape, where a call is every operator the bare pass meets (by its own rule), every interaction the tape serves, every non-aten entry point and every host-compute region; each gap opens after a synchronise outside the clock, so no wait for the device is in it; each run first times 64 back-to-back one-element calls under the same stack and takes their median gap, the instrument's own time per interval, off every interval; one untimed run, then at least 7 timed runs and more until the median cell total of all n runs is within 1% of the first n − 2's, at most 21 (a cell that does not settle is labelled, not refused); per layer the median net gaps, added over the layer's call positions in proportion to the null pass's gaps as before. It replaces the span-less-bare-prices method of 1 Nov 2026 and its device-behind label; the spread tolerance (T4.1 row 34) is unchanged. Step 5's summary now keys each cell by arm, density, length and batch (the density was missing, so a 0.25 cell's residual showed the 0.5 cell's). (2) **A memset's device time is priced by its realised bytes**: a memset no device activity answers set zero bytes, and zero bytes is zero device time; its host cost stays the measured API call's. The sensitivity bound (T4.1 row 31) prices a flagged memset at its realised size, zero, so it adds nothing where T3.2 recorded each one; it was 0.35 ms per 32768-token cell under the old rule (the largest size its operator's recorded memsets had). A size unknown for want of a trace still takes the zoo's largest. (3) **`masked_fill_` was mispriced** (+116% and +120% against trace in leg 2): an eager boolean-mask write `x[x == v] = s` is one `masked_fill_` kernel, and the CUDA graph serves it with the tape's stand-in, an `index_put_`, one kernel for one; the structural match paired the two as a variant, so the position took the stand-in's time (3.2 to 4.0 times its traced time in every oracle, mean-pool and XAttention cell; `masked_fill_` after a copy matched its trace within 3%). A variant match at an operator the tape substitutes for a boolean mask (`replay.STAND_IN_ATEN_OPS`: the substituted operators but `copy_`, since a graph's copy kernel does the copy) is now priced from the isolated replay of the original operators, which these arms already run. (4) **The P1 runner ends itself**: `t31_session.py` refuses a session on the card outside `scripts/session_exit.sh` (sync of logs and manifests to the bucket, then the halt, on any exit), as `t61_session.py` does; a CPU dry run under the wrapper passed, failed by a simulated exception, was killed and was refused, and each time synced and then halted; the teardown pulls from the bucket when the home logs are gone. (5) **One measurement added: the traced pass** (Section 5.3 step 8), after each cell's whole untraced measurement in T3.1's ground-truth step (`ground_truth.time_with_profiler_overhead`, as Shakedown 2 ran it), its result and per-arm overhead written to `traced_L*_b*.json` and never to ground truth; it does not change an untraced row and lengthens the ground-truth step. The block drift bound became a flag (below). Nothing else in pricing or measurement changed. Differences from `3f50aab` (T3.2a and T3.2b) that are orchestration or analysis only: the exit-wrapper refusal, the repeat label `c`, the step-5 summary's keys, Shakedown 2's step 6 (`t227_steps.py`) running the isolated original on the same rule, the not-measured oracle 0.5 cell at 32768 declared in the config (it was dropped before too), left-to-right sums in the baselines (exact recomputation across Python versions), the P2 and P4 runner's lock-file values and step layout, and the Gate A, repeats and training-file analyses. Not in the identity: T3.1's steps hold no delay injection (T2.25 is built and tested on a CPU, with no GPU step), so Gate A's leg 3 is not measured by a session on this identity.

- **8 Nov 2026.** **The resolution floor's form** (T4.1 row 16; adviser's ruling E of 8 Nov 2026, fourth paste), written before any scored share under it is computed. Where 19.42 ms came from: the 0.95 quantile of every clean repeat's absolute deviation from its series median, pooled over every series (every arm and cell, both P1 repeats, T3.2a and T3.2b; `lock/floors.py`, `resolution_floor`). The criterion, declared before it was applied: over the dense series (6 cells in each repeat, 12 series), the slope of log(the series' 0.95 quantile of absolute deviations) on log(the series median); at least 0.75 makes the floor relative, at most 0.25 absolute, anything between per length. Measured: slope 0.354 (standard error 0.067): the spread grows with the prefill, but much less than in proportion (the 0.95 deviation is 6.3 to 6.7 ms at 2048, 5.2% and 5.4% of the prefill, and 26.4 to 28.8 ms at 32768, 0.66% and 0.67%). **The rule: the resolution floor is per (context length, batch)**, the floors quantile of the pooled absolute deviations of every series at that cell only (every arm, the P1 repeat sessions; `floors.resolution_floors_by_length`); a difference at a cell inside its cell's floor is not interpreted, and Gate A v2's leg-2 exemption reads the same cell floor. The dense control's in-session drift flag is unchanged: it reads the session floor `dense_select` derives, so the code identity is not touched. The between-session differences of the dense medians are shown beside it, not used (1.7 to 2.3 ms at 2048 and 4096, 48 to 414 ms at 8192 to 32768, 0.6% to 6.7%). The pooled 19.42 ms is kept as the provisional single value only until lock.

- **9 Nov 2026.** **Gate A v2 on T3.2c** (T3.3; adviser's rulings 5 of the fifth 8 Nov 2026 paste and 9 of the sixth; `docs/t32c_gate_a_v2.md`, `docs/t32c_leg1_filters.md`). It does not pass as written. **Leg 1 cannot be applied:** it needs every cell in each repeat on the revised identity, and T3.2c is the only such repeat. Read on T3.2c alone, under the drift filters written before it (above), the recursion's median absolute relative error is below the sum's and the step form's in five of six readings (3.33% against 50.3% and 4.81% over all 35 cells with the relative filter or none; 6.12% against 55.8% and 7.14% over the 27 complete cells as recorded). With the 2048 group dropped (relative filter, 10 rounds) it is not: 3.12% against 2.87%. In every reading every predictor overpredicts the oracle 2.2 to 6.6 times: step 5's idle-device residual adds 5.4 to 19.0 s to oracle cells whose measured prefill is 1.4 to 2.9 s. **Leg 2 fails** at the 0.25 bound (provisional) in 3 of 35 cells, all oracle: `mm` and `block_sparse_attn_forward` at 16384 and 32768. Its compute-bound kernels trace 14 to 29% faster than their replay, while its memory-bound kernels agree within 1% and the other arms' same kernels within 9%; read as mispricing by clock state (the oracle leaves the device 12 to 18% idle under the power cap), not attribution and not profiler distortion. Scored share: 13.0% of pairs and 73.5% of priced device time under the T3.2a/b/c floors; 29.4% and 98.6% under the T3.2c-only floors of the rule above, where the same three cells fail. **Leg 3 fails** on `event_synchronize`, pooled slope 0.386 against [1/√2, √2]: T2.5 prices it as `Event.synchronize()` on a completed event (1.16 µs idle), while it is `record()` then `synchronize()`, a device round trip (8.59 µs measured). No `event_synchronize` occurs in any P1 cell, so a corrected price changes no P1 prediction. The other seven mechanisms are within range (1.035 to 1.067). No threshold was moved. Spend after T3.2c: ₹4,179 (21 table rows summing to ₹4,167, plus the second FA3 build's ₹12 estimate), headroom ₹9,821 of ₹14,000 (`docs/spend_ledger.md`). The decision stays the researcher's.
- **9 Nov 2026.** **The oracle's residual, diagnosed; the primary residual method for T3.2d, declared before launch** (adviser's ruling 2 of 9 Nov 2026, seventh paste; `docs/t32d_oracle_residual.md`). Read from T3.2c's nine oracle `layer_residual.json` (the bucket read logged in `docs/permission_log.md`). **Cause (H1, the instrument on):** the oracle builds each layer's mask on the host inside the prefill (`model._mask_from_scores`, the reference builder: a Python loop of 26 to 8,096 CPU assignments per layer), and step 5 ran it inside the residual's gaps with the tape's torch-function mode and the gap clock's dispatch mode active, so each CPU operator paid both modes' dispatch. The residual per layer is 9.0 to 679.5 ms against a measured 4.8 to 104 ms for the whole layer (1.9 to 6.5 times it), and it follows the assignments (the two densities at 16384 give about 66 µs per assignment, which predicts 32768 within 4%), not the device time. On the Mac the two modes slow the builder 8.0 to 13.0 times. **H2 rejected:** the scores are on the host, the builder touches no device, every gap opens after a synchronise, and a wait would follow device time, not the assignments. **H3 rejected:** the builder's CPU operators are in no other price (the bare pass prices device calls; T3.1's oracle sequence has no host-compute position). The idle-device method holds no device wait here; what contaminates it is the instrument's own dispatch, which every arm pays on an operator a gap does not meet (the other arms' residuals, 57 to 412 ms, are not diagnosed). **The fix** (`layer_residual`, schema 3, `idle_device_gaps_bare_host`): each declared host-side function (`cpu_boundary.HOST_SIDE_FUNCTIONS`) runs with every Python mode suspended, its own time added to its layer's gap and recorded beside it; not on the CPU boundary, where the host is the device. **Whole-prefill effect**, the committed components re-run with each oracle layer's residual divided by the Mac's inflation at its size: the oracle's recursion error falls from +120% to +558% to +31% and +33% at 2048, +11% at 4096 and +1% to +5% from 8192 on (16384 at 0.25: 5,473 ms to 1,450 ms against 1,399 measured; 32768: 19,166 ms to 2,933 ms against 2,913). With the residual removed entirely the figures move by at most 1.3 points, so the inflation factor's uncertainty does not matter. **Primary method for T3.2d: schema 3, for every arm.** Resolved inside the time-box, so the schema 2 method is not recorded beside it.
- **9 Nov 2026.** **Clocks: what T3.2c recorded, a correction, and the kernel pricing protocol for T3.2d, declared before launch** (adviser's rulings 3 and 4b of 9 Nov 2026, seventh paste). T3.2c sampled NVML in step 9's timed rounds only, at 10 Hz, per block: the SM clock's minimum and maximum, the memory clock and the throttle reasons; no power, no mean clock, and nothing in steps 5 and 6. Every block shows `sw_power_cap` and the memory clock at 6,251 MHz. **Correction to the Gate A v2 line above and to `docs/t32c_leg1_filters.md` §4:** at 16384 the rows' peak SM clocks are 1,995 MHz for dense, 1,950 to 1,980 for mean-pool and 1,980 to 2,025 for the oracle; the "1,215 to 1,410 MHz for dense and mean-pool" given there is wrong. Peak clocks do not separate the oracle, so the clock-state reading of leg 2's three failures is untested, not supported; the device-idle shares (oracle 82 to 88%, others 93 to 104%) stand. **The protocol:** steps 5 and 6 sample the SM and memory clocks, board power and throttle bits at 10 Hz from a separate process, per cell and per phase (`attnbench/pricing_clocks.py`); step 6 runs the captured replay back to back, each replay synchronised, for 5 s and at least 2 replays before the one profiled replay it prices from (`t31_steps.soak`), so the priced replay finds the sustained power-capped state of the timed rounds' back-to-back prefills. **What it can and cannot explain:** it prices the timed rounds' state; it does not by itself explain the oracle's 0.29 to 0.38 errors, whose reference is step 5's traced prefill, where the oracle's host-bound builder leaves the device idle between kernels. The declared test on T3.2d: for each failing family, its replay-versus-trace error against the ratio of the SM clock medians of step 5's `record` phase and step 6's `profiled_replay` phase. Leg 2's bound and families are unchanged. The timed ground-truth path does not import the new sampler.

T2.5 also measures `event_record_synchronize`, an event recorded and waited for on every call (`link.RECORD_THEN_WAIT`). From the recorded trace's CUDA calls in the prefill region, an event synchronise takes that price when a `cudaEventRecord` lies after the previous synchronising call (a stream, device or event synchronise, an implicit one, or a blocking copy), and the completed-event price otherwise (`attnbench/components/event_rule.py`; step 6 records the rule per cell, step 7 applies it). The delay injector records before every wait, so step 12's plan prices its `event_synchronize` at the round trip. **Effect on P1: 0 ms in every cell**: no P1 cell on T3.2c has an event synchronise. It changes leg 3's `event_synchronize` predictions only.

## Appendix B. Glossary

**Arm:** one way of producing masks (or dense attention), timed and scored identically. **Estimator:** the part of an arm that decides which blocks to keep. **Density:** fraction of blocks kept; *executed* density can differ from *mask* density after unions. **Crossover:** the grid bracket in which the sign of sparse minus dense time changes on a pairing (Section 5.4); there may be none or two. **Noise floor:** the between-session spread of end-to-end time (P1 repeats). **Resolution floor:** the smallest sparse-minus-dense difference interpreted, set from the within-session spread of that difference. **Regret:** how much slower the dispatcher's chosen configuration is than the best measured admissible one. **Graph break:** a point where `torch.compile` stops one compiled graph and falls back to Python before starting the next. **Pairing:** one host CPU platform with one GPU model on one provider. **Mask-rule era:** a period during which the mask rule was unchanged; results from different eras are not pooled.
